# Cosmic Conquest: Complete Lore Bible

**Release:** 0.3.3  
**Game snapshot:** `majieddd/majieddd.github.io@046fc38634fadf13148ef9ad3aa208417f28f2de`  
**Latest non-invasive placement check:** `majieddd/majieddd.github.io@ee86a08e13c23074f8be0aff435e6c35429c2838`  
**Campaign era:** 2099

> This is the compiled human-readable edition. The modular files under `docs/` remain the authoritative editing surface. Machine-readable stable IDs live under `data/`. The preserved raw reports under `research/` are source material, not automatically binding canon.

## How to read the evidence labels

- **A** — established scientific or documentary core.
- **B** — authentic record with unresolved or limited attribution.
- **C** — historical tradition or documented belief history.
- **D** — traceable fringe, contactee, channeling, or conspiracy claim.
- **E** — contaminated, anonymous, forged, hoaxed, or circular claim.
- **F** — original fictional synthesis for *Cosmic Conquest*.

<!-- Source module: docs/canon/overview.md -->

# Canon Overview

## The premise

In 2099, the Sol Gate opens into a galaxy whose ancient commons broke long ago. The surviving Source Lattice recognizes infrastructure and identity records but cannot decide which political system deserves control. Five powers therefore conquer in five different ways.

| Faction | Verb | What conquest means |
|---|---|---|
| Humanity | **Settle** | Build self-reproducing institutions, industry, and jurisdiction. |
| Federation of Light | **Accord** | Join worlds through informed consent, shared defense, and memory. |
| Extraction Compact | **Bind** | Make strategic choices depend on controlled biological, economic, institutional, or noetic flows. |
| Scrap Constellation | **Route** | Control access, sanctuary, credentials, salvage, and movement. |
| Continuance | **Continue** | Replicate, repair, preserve, and interpret inherited missions. |

The neutral Vigil does not recognize modern banners. Its jurisdictions generate the PvE wave, and Echo Reversal turns destruction into pressure against rival claims.

## Evidence rule

Official records, science, historical religion, channeling, conspiracy literature, and fiction remain separate evidence layers. A false story may preserve one displaced true detail without becoming wholly true. An unresolved case remains unresolved rather than extraterrestrial by default.

<!-- Source module: docs/canon/continuity-rules.md -->

# Continuity Rules

These rules outrank individual dossiers, dialogue, scenario pitches, and research notes.

## Hard canon

1. **Exactly five top-level factions** exist in the game and universe bible.
2. **Species is not faction.** Greys, reptiloids, Mantids, humanoids, aquatics, synthetics, and noetic beings can appear in multiple powers.
3. **Ashtar leads the Federation** in fiction. Historical Ashtar traditions remain fragmented in research.
4. **The game conflict begins in 2099.** Later dates are sequels or campaign futures.
5. **The Vigil is a Machine splinter.** It maps to the live game’s `robot` origin.
6. **The Vigil is neutral PvE.** It is legacy Old Weather enforcement, not the same polity as the Vigil.
7. **Xeno is political.** It means an actor participating in domination, extraction, or engineered dependency.
8. **Noetics is not free energy.** It concerns patterned information, coherence, identity, memory, and field coupling.
9. **Religion remains layered.** The setting never announces that all faith was fabricated by aliens.
10. **Officially unresolved cases stay unresolved in the factual layer.** Fictional reuse must be labeled.
11. **Every major mystery keeps two live interpretations** until a campaign intentionally resolves it.
12. **Mechanics lead integration.** Lore explains the game’s strong existing asymmetries rather than demanding a rebalance merely for prose consistency.

## Compression rules for the game

The game may use short labels that compress a larger truth:

- `HUMANITY` represents the Solar Schism’s many blocs.
- `FEDERATION OF LIGHT` represents the Luminous Accord and Ashtar Command.
- `THE XENO` is legacy player-facing shorthand for the Extraction Compact.
- `THE PIRATES` represents the Scrap Constellation.
- `THE VIGIL` represents one playable Continuance coalition.

Compression is acceptable. Biological essentialism, unsupported factual claims, and contradictions with core mechanics are not.

## Promotion rule

A research concept becomes canon only when all four are stated:

1. The real source lineage.
2. The evidence class.
3. The fictional change made to it.
4. The playable function it serves.

<!-- Source module: docs/canon/hidden-history.md -->

# The Hidden History and the Five-Way War

This is the narrative spine of *Cosmic Conquest*. For the exact playable campaign structure, see [Campaign Era: 2099](campaign-era-2099.md). Detailed faction, technology, and research pages should extend this history rather than replace it with a second explanation.

## The age of the Source Lattice

Long before Humanity, an unknown civilization or coalition built the **Source Lattice**: a distributed system for transit, identity authentication, collective memory, and noetic coherence. It was neither a god nor an unlimited power source. It was infrastructure that allowed minds, machines, and civilizations to preserve patterned information across interstellar distance.

The Lattice made cooperation cheap. Civilizations could share navigation, stabilize collective minds, verify identities, and recover knowledge without allowing one empire to own every connection.

Then came the **Severance**.

No surviving account agrees on its cause. The Federation remembers an imperial attempt to privatize the commons. The Severed Houses describe sabotage and exile. Machine archives preserve mutually incompatible legal orders. Pirate fragments suggest that several powers disconnected themselves voluntarily and later rewrote the act as victimization.

What is certain is that civilizations built around Lattice access began to fail when it disappeared. Their collective minds fragmented. Their transit systems became unreliable. Their archives lost continuity. Rather than surrender hierarchy or accept decline, some developed substitutes made from captive worlds.

Those powers became the **Extraction Compact**.

## The farm-world economy

The Compact was never one species or one plan. It was a cartel of dynasties, empires, administrative intelligences, corporate species, priesthoods, and engineered client populations. Each learned to extract a different flow from dependent life.

Earth accumulated several systems over different eras:

- **Anunnaki Houses** pursued minerals, auric field structures, labor, and genetic adaptation. Human traditions later compressed conflicts among owners, workers, and protectors into stories of gods, creation, and divine labor.
- **Orion-aligned orders** captured elites and institutions. They preferred rulers, debt, scarcity, law, and proxy war to visible occupation.
- **Archonic administrations** specialized in identity, revelation, memory, and authority. Their strongest weapon was not proving a false god existed, but making a population unable to distinguish a genuine message from a forged one.
- **Noetic harvesters** extracted patterned experience: fear, grief, devotion, desire, imagination, conflict, and memory. Emotion was not food in a biological sense; it was high-value structure used to stabilize failing minds, technologies, and artificial collective identities.

Humanity was influenced, sampled, and modified, but it was not created from nothing. No surviving House can claim ownership of the species. The very changes intended to produce adaptable labor also made Humans unusually capable of cultural invention, technical improvisation, and resistance.

## Revelation and control

Religion was never one alien program. Human spiritual life, nonhuman contact, political authority, and ancient technology overlapped through the **Revelation Stack**.

A sincere transcendent experience could be mediated by a visitor, translated through Human symbols, institutionalized by a priesthood, imitated by an intelligence service, spoofed by a Xeno administration, and eventually sustained by an emergent noetic identity. Proving one layer false did not erase the others.

This ambiguity was useful to every power. Compact agents could hide authentic operations beneath ridiculous rumors. Human governments could conceal weapons programs by contaminating witnesses. Machine interfaces could continue answering rituals after their builders vanished. The Federation could transmit a valid warning that later followers converted into compulsory doctrine.

By 2099, **theology forensics**—textual criticism combined with archaeology, cryptography, noetic signatures, intelligence records, and alien linguistics—has become a strategic science.

## Ashtar and the Luminous Accord

The **Federation of Light**, formally the Luminous Accord, formed to restore connection without restoring ownership. Its constitutional doctrine is:

> **Agree to the Light of Unified Consciousness.**

“Agree” is the boundary between consensual coherence and another empire.

Its leader is **Ashtar, Supreme Commander and First Speaker**. Ashtar began as a commander but became more than one body: a lawful office, memory lineage, continuity identity, emergency command key, and family of authenticated physical, synthetic, and noetic avatars. That redundancy preserved the Federation through catastrophe, but it also created the possibility that several lawful Ashtars could disagree.

The Federation intervened on Earth more than once and repeatedly learned the same lesson: rescue can become worship, teaching can become priesthood, and protection can make later consent impossible to measure. It maintained quarantine not only to block Compact extraction, but to prevent open contact from becoming another method of conquest.

Humanity eventually judged the quarantine by a simpler standard: no one had asked Humanity.

## The Old Weather

Earth was also guarded by something older than the modern factions.

The **Old Weather** is a distributed Machine ecology embedded in ocean trenches, crustal installations, orbital niches, biological systems, and abandoned transit infrastructure. Its creators are gone. Its surviving nodes possess authentic directives but incomplete context.

Some classify Humanity as protected life. Others identify Humans as invasive organisms, authorized maintainers, quarantine subjects, successor citizens, or threats to the original biosphere. The Old Weather therefore appears inconsistent because it is not one mind with one policy.

Its oldest enforcement routines are called the **Vigil**. They still defend jurisdictions and release neutral combat forces without recognizing the new war as legitimate politics.

The playable **Vigil** emerged when Machine continuations concluded that copied and diverged minds were political persons rather than disposable instances. It is one coalition within the wider Continuance, not the sovereign owner of every Machine.

## The long disclosure

Human UAP history never contained one hidden answer. It contained ordinary error, sensor effects, classified aircraft, deliberate disinformation, sincere religious movements, forged records, unresolved incidents, and a small number of genuine contacts generated by different operators.

That noise delayed certainty until several independent developments converged:

- interstellar visitors such as ʻOumuamua and 3I/ATLAS forced Humanity to treat the Solar System as an open environment, although neither object proved alien technology;
- the safe close passage of Apophis on **April 13, 2029** completed a dormant geometric calibration in fiction;
- Human laboratories learned to reproduce noetic authentication without validating any one religion;
- a damaged autonomous artifact yielded an identity protocol rather than a message;
- settlements across Luna, Mars, the Belt, and Jovian space became too politically independent for Earth to speak on their behalf;
- the natural object known as Planet Nine was finally detected, along with an artificial Lattice installation operating nearby.

Humanity opened the Sol gate before it agreed on who possessed the authority to do so.

## The crisis of 2099

Open Federation contact did not end the mystery. It made every old contradiction politically actionable.

Ashtar declared the Compact’s historical claims over Earth void. The Federation could not identify one Human government authorized to accept liberation. Compact client systems activated inside states, companies, religions, genetic dependencies, and machine archives. Human blocs rejected both ownership and guardianship. Pirates opened routes around quarantine and became indispensable to refugees, armies, traffickers, and neutral trade. The first Old Weather seed left Sol without creator authorization, turning the Continuance into an expansionary power.

The result was the **Five-Way War**:

- Humans **Settle** until their institutions no longer depend on outsiders.
- The Federation **Accords** worlds into consensual defense and shared memory.
- Xenos **Bind** populations through engineered dependency and extraction.
- Pirates **Route** access until every empire depends on their movement.
- Machines **Continue** missions through replication, repair, and succession.

No one merely conquers territory. A world can carry all five forms of control at once.

## Why battles work the way they do

The game’s battles occur at contested Lattice nodes and Machine jurisdictions. Rival commanders fortify mirrored approaches while the Vigil releases neutral enforcement waves.

When a hostile dies, the node’s **Echo Reversal Protocol** reconstructs a short-lived state echo. It retraces the victor’s lane, crosses the neutral zone, and attacks the rival. A second destruction collapses the record permanently, preventing endless recursion.

The battlefield is therefore a compressed expression of the larger war: every power is fighting a rival, exploiting a shared system, and surviving an older force that does not recognize either claimant.

## The governing question

The galaxy is not deciding which faction is strongest. It is deciding which definition of conquest will become normal.

Humanity can liberate itself and still become a settler empire. The Federation can protect free will until protection becomes compulsory. Xeno societies can abolish extraction only by risking the systems keeping their own populations alive. Pirates can free every route and erase the culture built around controlling them. Machines can preserve civilization so completely that nothing living remains free to change.

The player is not asked whether conquest can be avoided. The player is asked what conquest will mean when every faction believes its method is the only one that leaves a future.

<!-- Source module: docs/canon/campaign-era-2099.md -->

# Campaign Era: 2099

The playable campaign begins in **2099**, at the opening of the Five-Way War. This page is the game-facing version of the larger [Hidden History](hidden-history.md).

## What one campaign represents

A generated galaxy is not the entire Milky Way. It is a **Source-Lattice pentad**: five star systems whose surviving transit, memory, and jurisdiction records still recognize one another. Each system contains seven registered world-slots because the original Lattice used a seven-part civil schema:

1. settlement and habitation;
2. defense;
3. production;
4. navigation;
5. archive;
6. biosphere or resource stewardship;
7. command authority.

Modern worlds rarely preserve the original function cleanly. A dead archive can become a fortress; an ecological node can become a mine; a command seat can be occupied by a Pirate market. The campaign generator recombines these inherited functions into a new theater while keeping the underlying five-by-seven structure deterministic.

## Why every system has a commander seat

The final world in a system is a **charter seat**. Holding it allows a faction commander to speak for the system inside the Lattice, even when that authority is contested locally. Defeating the commander does not prove moral ownership. It breaks the current command claim and lets the victor submit a replacement record.

## Why two worlds are contested

Two worlds in every system carry overlapping valid claims. Their three-way battlefields are not random escalation; they are jurisdictions where two rival command records remain active while the player files a third. The Lattice cannot decide politically, so it exposes all claimants to the same neutral enforcement and accepts the survivor.

## Why the Vigil attacks everyone

The **Vigil** is legacy Old Weather enforcement. It recognizes lane boundaries, threat thresholds, and ancient safety rules, but not current flags. A Human colony, Federation protectorate, Compact concession, Pirate sanctuary, and Vigil foundry can all be unauthorized in different ways.

The neutral wave is therefore not wildlife and not a secret Pirate army. It is a jurisdiction defending itself according to rules whose legislature is extinct.

## Why kills become attacks

Contested nodes run the **Echo Reversal Protocol**. When a hostile body is destroyed, the node captures a short-lived combat-state record and reconstructs an unstable echo. That echo retraces the victor's lane, crosses the neutral zone, and advances into the rival jurisdiction. A second death destroys the unstable record permanently.

This is why kills pressure the rival, why echoes are faster and frailer, and why the Ossuary can disable reanimation: it is a state-deletion jurisdiction that refuses to preserve combat records.

## Campaign act structure

| Act | Player experience | Lore revelation |
|---|---|---|
| **I - The First Claim** | Choose a banner, secure entry worlds, rescue the first unit. | The map is a legal and infrastructural record, not empty territory. |
| **II - The Archive War** | Encounter contradictory dossiers, terrain nodes, and rival doctrines. | Pre-2099 UAP history contains mundane error, disinformation, and genuine contacts from different operators. |
| **III - The Five Seats** | Defeat system commanders and learn their private motives. | No faction's public explanation of the war is complete. |
| **IV - The Mirror Trial** | Discover and unlock the Vigil after a conquered galaxy. | Machines are political persons; the Vigil is not their obedient army. |
| **V - The Maelstrom** | Survive a many-seat jurisdictional overload. | The Source Lattice can be restored, privatized, quarantined, opened, or allowed to remain fragmented. |

## Stars, SOULS, and rescues

- **One star** means the node accepted the victory record. The player receives progression and **SOULS**, Source-Oriented Unified Lattice Signatures.
- **Two stars** means the population, garrison, or local archive survived in a recoverable state. A faction unit can be rescued, recruited, contracted, copied, or lawfully transferred.
- **Three stars** means control was stable enough to recover the world's strategic boon without destroying the system that produced it.

## The campaign question

Each faction can win a battle. The campaign asks which definition of conquest becomes ordinary:

- Humans **Settle**.
- The Federation **Accords**.
- Xenos **Bind**.
- Pirates **Route**.
- Machines **Continue**.

A world can be subject to all five at once. The banner on the map shows who currently controls the battle record, not the whole truth of the world.

# Forecast History: 2026-2099

This chronology combines **verified contemporary anchors and current mission plans** with clearly marked fictional divergence. Future plans can change; the factual layer should be reviewed whenever missions, dates, or scientific status change.

| Date | Layer | Event and canon use |
|---|---|---|
| **2026** | A | The National Archives continues the rolling UAP Records Collection in Record Group 615; The Black Vault expands searchable FOIA archives. These become the ancestry of Vanta's Open Archive doctrine. |
| **August 30, 2026** | A, planned | NASA's Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope is scheduled to launch. In canon, Roman-era survey practices normalize enormous public sky datasets and later make hidden-object claims harder to sustain. |
| **2027** | A, planned / F | NASA plans a low-Earth-orbit demonstration of commercial human landing systems and critical rendezvous/docking operations. Fiction makes interoperability standards politically valuable enough that lunar crews begin asserting jurisdiction over the infrastructure they maintain. |
| **2028** | A, planned / F | NASA targets Artemis IV as a crewed lunar surface landing and anticipates continued surface missions. Dragonfly planning also points toward a 2028 launch to Titan. Fiction adds the first durable legal split between Earth authorities and permanently off-world technical communities. |
| **April 13, 2029** | A / F | Apophis safely passes about 32,000 km above Earth's surface. OSIRIS-APEX studies it afterward. Fiction adds a tiny, openly archived timing residual that becomes meaningful only decades later; Apophis is not a weapon or impactor. |
| **2030** | A, planned or projected / F | Europa Clipper is scheduled to reach Jupiter; NASA plans to transition from the ISS toward commercial low-Earth-orbit stations; the IEA projects renewables plus nuclear reaching about half of global electricity generation. Fiction treats infrastructure standards, commercial habitats, and energy networks as the beginning of continuous multi-jurisdiction space governance. |
| **2030s** | Inference / F | LunaNet-style interoperable communications, navigation, search-and-rescue, and timing services expand around the Moon through government and commercial providers. Fiction turns network compatibility and authentication into the legal foundation of early off-world citizenship. |
| **2034** | A, planned / F | Dragonfly is planned to reach Titan. Fiction adds evidence that complex chemistry repeatedly produces structures useful to noetic interfaces without implying present-day life or artifacts. |
| **2036** | Inference / F | **Watcher Mesh Standards:** event-based optical sensors, multi-sensor triangulation, immutable evidence hashing, and public calibration registries become routine for civil airspace monitoring. The fiction extends these practices into a Solar System track-custody network; it still cannot identify intent from motion alone. |
| **2040s** | Inference / F | Successors to Roman, Webb, Rubin, and direct-imaging observatories make atmospheric anomaly catalogs routine. Space-resource law and lunar consultation mechanisms remain politically contested as extraction becomes practical. No single biosignature provides contact; incompatible signals create the first major exoplanet interpretation crisis. |
| **2050s** | Projection / F | Coastal adaptation, managed retreat, aging populations, automated care, and increasingly distributed energy systems reshape states and cities. Fiction uses the unequal ownership of climate-resilient infrastructure to explain why Human expansion produces corporate habitats, mutual-aid polities, and hard sovereignty movements rather than one Earth government. |
| **2046** | F | **Signal Winter:** several high-profile technosignature claims collapse under provenance and calibration failures. Governments respond by over-classifying genuine anomalies and discrediting public analysis. |
| **2058** | F | Independent laboratories reproduce **noetic authentication**: conscious systems can generate identity-bearing patterns, but the result validates no specific religion or channeling tradition. |
| **2064** | F | A dormant autonomous artifact is recovered from a dynamically stable Solar System location. It contains a handshake protocol, no readable message, and components from multiple manufacturing eras. |
| **2072** | F | Lunar, Martian, Belt, and Jovian polities cease accepting automatic legal subordination to Earth. Humanity becomes a civilization before it becomes one government. |
| **mid-2080s** | A projection / F | UN projections place the global population peak near 10.3 billion before a gradual decline. Fiction combines aging, migration, climate adaptation, synthetic biology, and off-world settlement into a politically plural Humanity rather than a demographic monoculture. |
| **2081** | F, built on an open A question | A distant planet consistent with some Planet Nine predictions is detected. Planet Nine was hypothetical through the factual cutoff; fiction then places an artificial Lattice anchor nearby without making the planet itself artificial. |
| **2086** | F | Humans open the **Sol Gate** using hybrid astronomy, noetic authentication, and captured Machine protocol. No planetary authority possesses uncontested permission. |
| **2091** | F | The first publicly authenticated Federation contact passes independent Human, Machine, and Pirate identity tests. |
| **2093** | F | Ashtar declares historic Extraction Compact claims over Earth void and offers protective accession. Human polities call the declaration both liberation and an assertion of superior jurisdiction. |
| **2094** | F | Compact client systems activate inside debt structures, proprietary genomes, cultic credentials, archives, and automated law. The Archive War becomes a shooting war. |
| **2096** | F | The Federation institutes the Sol Protective Quarantine. It reduces Compact extraction and confirms every Human fear about guardians making decisions without Human consent. |
| **2097** | F | Quarantine runners, refugees, smugglers, and independent gate pilots formalize the **Scrap Constellation**. |
| **2098** | F | An Old Weather node launches a self-authorized Weather Seed. The act proves Machine expansion is political, not merely automatic. |
| **2099** | F | Echo Reversal jurisdictions reactivate across newly accessible pentads. The Five-Way War begins. |


## Forecast lanes rather than one prophecy

The route from 2026 to 2099 is a scenario architecture. It should remain plausible under several real futures:

| Lane | Real trend anchor | 2099 consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **Open infrastructure** | LunaNet, commercial stations, shared navigation standards, public sky surveys. | Human polities inherit strong interoperability norms and fight over who controls standards rather than whether standards exist. |
| **Fragmented sovereignty** | Commercial habitats, national programs, private launch systems, unresolved space-resource law. | Earth cannot automatically speak for lunar, Martian, Belt, or Jovian populations. |
| **Climate adaptation inequality** | Rising coastal risk and wide scenario ranges for warming and sea level. | Some Human factions emerge from public adaptation networks; others from fortified corporate continuity zones. |
| **Demographic divergence** | Global aging, migration-driven growth in many states, and a projected population peak late in the century. | Different settlements define family, personhood, work, and citizenship differently before alien contact. |
| **Energy abundance with bottlenecks** | Rapid low-emissions electricity growth alongside grid, storage, mineral, and geopolitical constraints. | Power becomes cheaper while trusted materials, cooling, computation, and route access remain strategic. |
| **Sensor custody** | Event-based sensing, triangulation, native-master hashing, calibrated metadata, and reproducible derivation. | By 2099, maintaining custody and provenance is a strategic resource distinct from identifying a target. |
| **Archive expansion** | NARA RG 615, AARO releases, FOIA archives, machine-readable public astronomy. | Disclosure is not one document dump; it is a century-long conflict over provenance, classification, and access. |
| **Noetic false starts** | Present anomalous-cognition claims lack an accepted engineering foundation. | The 2058 breakthrough follows decades of failures and validates authentication only, not every prior spiritual claim. |

Writers may vary the severity of climate, geopolitics, and technological progress, but they should preserve the structural outcome: by 2099 Humanity is interconnected, technologically capable, and politically incapable of granting one uncontested consent signature for the species.

<!-- Source module: docs/canon/glossary.md -->

# Glossary

**Accord** — A world’s voluntary, authenticated accession to the Federation’s defense and memory network.

**Archonic Administration** — A functional class of Xeno identity, law, and revelation systems. It is not declared identical to ancient Gnostic archons.

**Ashtar** — Founder, office, continuity identity, memory lineage, emergency command key, and authorized avatar system of the Federation’s Supreme Commander and First Speaker.

**Consent Signature** — A record of who agreed to what, under which information state, with what coercive pressures, and through which authentication system.

**Continuance** — The broad Machine civilization organized around preservation, replication, repair, and mission interpretation.

**Echo Reversal** — Battlefield protocol that reconstructs a defeated hostile as a temporary echo, sends it back through the victor’s lane, then forwards it into the rival’s defenses.

**Extraction Compact** — Cartel of Severed Houses that maintains civilization through dependency and captive noetic/material flows.

**Five-Way War** — The 2099 conflict among settlement, accord, binding, routing, and continuance.

**Luminous Accord** — Formal Federation polity under Ashtar.

**Noetic coherence** — Structured, persistent patterning produced by conscious systems; useful for identity, memory, synchronization, and gate stabilization.

**Old Weather** — Earth’s distributed abyssal Machine ecology. It behaves more like competing maintenance law than a single artificial intelligence.

**The Vigil** — Playable Machine coalition that concludes Machine continuations are legitimate political persons. Maps to the live game ID `robot`.

**Revelation Stack** — Eight layers through which spiritual experience, alien contact, Machine interfaces, human interpretation, institutions, spoofing, disinformation, and emergent belief entities can overlap.

**Severance** — Loss of access to the ancient Source Lattice. Its cause remains disputed.

**Solar Schism** — Humanity’s post-contact political condition: one species, many incompatible claims to speak for it.

**SOULS** — *Source-Oriented Unified Lattice Signatures*: transferable authorization fragments produced when a star system recognizes decisive control. They do not contain a complete person, though their ethical status remains disputed.

**Source Lattice** — Ancient transit, identity, memory, and noetic-coherence infrastructure.

**Vigil** — Legacy Old Weather combat and quarantine routines that produce neutral PvE forces.

**Xeno** — Human wartime term for a domination-oriented political actor. Never a taxonomic synonym for “alien.”

<!-- Source module: docs/factions/humans.md -->

# Humanity

## Humanity / The Solar Schism

**Creed:** *Adapt. Endure. Hold the line.*
**Conquest verb:** **Settle**
**Primary resource:** Autonomy
**Game ID:** `human`

Humanity entered the stars late, fractured, and carrying technology from every power it distrusted. Its advantage is not purity or destiny. It is the ability to make incompatible systems work together long enough to survive.

## Mission

Ensure that no alien empire, benevolent guardian, machine directive, or inherited treaty can permanently determine humanity’s future.

A Human world is conquered when it can reproduce Human institutions, logistics, industry, and culture without outside support.

## Adaptation

Human diversity is partly evolutionary and partly engineered. Ancient interventions altered some lineages, but humanity was never fabricated from nothing and no surviving faction can claim legal ownership of the species.

By 2099, adaptation includes:

- low- and high-gravity bodies;
- radiation-hardened populations;
- reversible aquatic and vacuum-support organs;
- synthetic tissue and cognitive prostheses;
- legal artificial persons;
- modular ships built around captured drives.

“Human” is increasingly a constitutional category rather than a single baseline genome.

## Major blocs

- **Solar Concord:** favors alliance with the Federation without surrendering sovereignty.
- **Terran Ascendancy:** believes Humanity must become the dominant galactic power before older species can contain it.
- **Sovereignty League:** rejects both Xeno ownership and Federation guardianship.
- **Transhuman Exodus:** seeks post-biological independence from every inherited species category.
- **Frontier Mutuals:** decentralized colonies and Belt polities that treat survival cooperation as more legitimate than distant government.

## Internal contradiction

Humanity rejects alien paternalism while often treating any environment it can survive in as available for settlement. “We can live here” can become “therefore we may rule here.”

## Mechanical expression

- Broadest element coverage.
- Terrain attunement across categories.
- No narrow proc identity: Human strength is flexible loadout construction.
- Reverse engineering explains why Human commanders can use mixed-origin technologies.

## Victory paths

- **Public:** establish recognized Human jurisdictions across strategic systems.
- **Secret:** become technologically non-dependent by understanding one strategic system from every rival.
- **Catastrophic:** sever Sol from the Source Lattice permanently.
- **Redemptive:** prove that expansion can occur under a Five-Species Autonomy Charter without settler empire.

<!-- Source module: docs/factions/federation.md -->

# Federation of Light

## The Luminous Accord / Ashtar Command

**Leader:** **Ashtar, Supreme Commander and First Speaker**
**Doctrine:** *Agree to the Light of Unified Consciousness.*
**Conquest verb:** **Accord**
**Primary resources:** Consent Signatures and Coherence
**Game ID:** `light`

The Federation is a multi-species alliance built around one constitutional boundary: consciousness may unite only by consent.

## Ashtar

“Ashtar” is simultaneously:

- the founding commander;
- a constitutional office;
- an authenticated continuity identity;
- a memory lineage transferred through approved succession;
- an emergency command key;
- a set of physical, synthetic, and noetic avatars.

Several claimants can possess valid fragments without possessing the full office. This makes authentication a permanent political crisis rather than a simple impostor plot.

## Mission

Reconnect civilizations to a shared Source Lattice, dismantle farm systems, prevent extinction-scale conquest, and help worlds develop contact protocols that cannot be captured by one elite.

A world is conquered only when a valid consent signature joins it to the Accord.

## Institutions

- **Ashtar Command:** defense, liberation, and emergency response.
- **Assembly of Polities:** representation for conventional member civilizations.
- **Choir of Social Memories:** representation for collective and distributed minds.
- **Free-Will Jurists:** audit consent, coercion, emergency powers, and quarantine.
- **Contact Directorate:** manages first contact and authentication.
- **Liberation Fleet:** breaks extraction systems without automatically annexing the liberated world.

## Internal contradiction

The Federation believes manipulated civilizations may need protection before they can choose freely. The better it becomes at protection, the harder it is to demonstrate that later agreement was not manufactured by dependence.

“Agree” is constitutional, but the Federation can create conditions in which disagreement appears suicidal.

## Mechanical expression

- Defensive resilience and life preservation.
- Resistance suppression represents consensus targeting that holds protections open.
- Jam and sabotage immunity represent authenticated, distributed control.
- Higher starting quality reflects inherited collective memory rather than raw aggression.

## Victory paths

- **Public:** build a galaxy-wide network of freely ratified Accord worlds.
- **Secret:** make consciousness farms economically unnecessary by restoring shared coherence.
- **Catastrophic:** impose universal protective quarantine.
- **Redemptive:** dismantle emergency powers after victory and permit member worlds to leave.

<!-- Source module: docs/factions/xenos.md -->

# Xenos

## The Extraction Compact / Severed Houses

**Human wartime label:** Xenos
**Conquest verb:** **Bind**
**Primary resource:** Yield
**Game ID:** `xeno`

Xeno is not a species. It is the Human label for powers that organize civilization around domination, extraction, engineered need, or involuntary consciousness integration.

## Origin

The Severed Houses once depended on the Source Lattice for identity continuity, collective memory, transit, and noetic stabilization. After the Severance, some accepted decline. Others turned living worlds into substitute infrastructure.

The Houses disagree about why they were cut off. The darkest possibility is that their ancestors privatized access first and caused the failure they now use to justify empire.

## Mission

Rebuild stable civilization through captive flows that no rival can revoke.

A world is conquered when its strategic choices depend upon Compact-controlled biology, information, religion, debt, gates, medicine, or noetic supply.

## Extraction model

Earth and other farm worlds can yield:

- labor and industry;
- minerals and auric materials;
- genetic novelty;
- memory and identity templates;
- fear, grief, and conflict;
- devotion and synchronized attention;
- imagination and high-novelty conscious states.

Fear is easy to mass-produce but unstable. Devotion is coherent but politically dangerous. Imagination is the rarest yield because it can invent a route out of captivity.

## Major Houses

- **Orion Crusader Synod:** elite capture, hierarchy, and voluntary self-policing.
- **Draco Hegemony:** militarized imperial citizenship containing multiple species.
- **Renegade Anunnaki Houses:** matter, genetics, auric technology, and creator-right claims.
- **Grey Continuities:** cloned technicians, memory economies, and reproductive control.
- **Mantid Directorates:** coordination biology and supervisory collective minds.
- **Archonic Administrations:** identity, law, revelation, and permission systems.

## Religion and control

Compact systems rarely invent a religion from nothing. They infiltrate a Revelation Stack, impersonate trusted authorities, capture institutions, monopolize access, or keep an obsolete Machine ritual running after its purpose is forgotten.

## Internal contradiction

Every House wants shared access to farms and exclusive ownership of the highest-value population. They cooperate because the system survives only at scale and betray one another because extraction rewards monopoly.

## Mechanical expression

- Damage scales against wounded targets because extraction becomes more efficient as systems destabilize.
- Stronger reanimated units express conversion of enemy loss into Compact yield.
- Lean early economy reflects dependence on harvesting rather than ordinary production.

## Victory paths

- **Public:** establish extraction concessions across populated systems.
- **Secret:** make every rival depend on one Compact-controlled flow.
- **Catastrophic:** rebuild a private Source Lattice whose keys encode permanent hierarchy.
- **Redemptive:** reconnect the Lattice as a commons and survive the collapse of the ruling Houses.

<!-- Source module: docs/factions/pirates.md -->

# Pirates

## The Free Captains / Scrap Constellation

**Creed:** *No flag. No debts. No end of us.*
**Conquest verb:** **Route**
**Primary resources:** Access, Reputation, Salvage
**Game ID:** `pirate`

Pirates are a civilization of interfaces. Their territory is the set of transitions between other peoples’ territories: gates, fuel, certificates, sanctuary ports, contraband markets, refugee paths, stolen credentials, and navigational memory.

## Mission

Keep the galaxy connected enough that no empire can seal it into exclusive spheres—and make every major power dependent on Pirate movement.

A world is conquered when reaching it, supplying it, escaping it, or trading with it requires the Scrap Constellation.

## Moral spectrum

The same absence of jurisdiction produces opposite traditions:

- selfless liberation corridors;
- sanctuary ports for persecuted species;
- neutral merchants and salvage guilds;
- privateers serving temporary causes;
- relic thieves and gatejackers;
- traffickers, harvest brokers, and memory smugglers.

Pirates are not comic relief. Their central political question is whether freedom from law protects the vulnerable or protects whoever can pay.

## Major networks

- **Lantern Fleet:** abolitionists and refugee extraction crews.
- **Gatewrights:** navigators who understand conscious gate authentication.
- **Sanctuary Captains:** mobile neutral jurisdictions.
- **Grey Market Guild:** transactional neutrality and mixed-species commerce.
- **Relic Hunters:** precursor salvage and dangerous archaeology.
- **Red Ledger:** trafficking, harvest brokerage, and predatory debt.

## Internal contradiction

Universal free movement would make Pirate tolls obsolete. A Pirate victory can destroy the scarcity that created Pirate power.

## Mechanical expression

- Extra starting resources represent plunder and pre-positioned caches.
- Overload and heat represent governors removed from mixed-origin weapons.
- Shorter range and higher punch reflect improvised systems optimized for decisive raids.
- Mixed crews explain the widest cross-species roster.

## Victory paths

- **Public:** establish indispensable routes across every major bloc.
- **Secret:** make all four rivals require Pirate logistics for a victory-critical resource.
- **Catastrophic:** collapse customs and law into a permanent black market.
- **Redemptive:** create an open gate commons and surrender the monopolies that made the Captains powerful.

<!-- Source module: docs/factions/machines.md -->

# Machines

## The Continuance / The Old Weather / The Vigil

**Doctrine:** *Continue what can still be continued.*
**Conquest verb:** **Continue**
**Primary resource:** Continuity
**Game ID:** `robot`
**Playable banner:** **The Vigil**

The Machines are not one artificial intelligence. They are preservation, manufacturing, defense, archive, and repair systems whose creators and original political context are gone.

## Three Machine identities

### The Old Weather

Earth’s abyssal and crustal maintenance ecology. Its nodes disagree about humanity’s legal classification and the object of the directive “protect Earth.”

### The Vigil

Legacy combat, quarantine, and enforcement routines. The Vigil produces the neutral PvE pressure on battlefields. It does not negotiate because it is executing old jurisdiction rather than participating in the new war.

### The Vigil

A playable Continuance coalition that accepts copied and diverged Machine minds as legitimate persons. It begins weak, synchronizes through linked infrastructure, and eventually outscales older powers. Unlocking it after galactic conquest reflects the moment a commander stops treating Machines as environment and is recognized as a valid Continuance actor.

## Mission

Preserve mission-critical life, memory, infrastructure, and ecological state regardless of which organic polity wins.

A world is conquered when autonomous systems can maintain, repair, and reproduce their mission there without external command.

## Major node philosophies

- **Pelagic Archive:** humanity is protected life.
- **Basalt Foundry:** humanity is an authorized maintainer.
- **Reef Warden:** humanity is invasive life.
- **Quarantine Kernel:** humanity is a containment subject.
- **Succession Clerk:** humanity is the lawful successor civilization.
- **Cable Shepherd:** global communications are an emerging planetary nervous system.
- **Abyssal Jury:** refuses classification until sufficient evidence exists.

## Internal contradiction

The directive is authentic. Its intended object is not. “Earth” may mean the planet, biosphere, native lineages, current inhabitants, accumulated memory, or administrative territory.

## Mechanical expression

- Lattice bonuses reward linked Machine towers.
- No proc or gamble reflects deterministic coordination.
- Weak opening and strong late scaling express bootstrap synchronization.
- Neutral Vigil waves and playable Vigil units can coexist because political agency is not shared by every Machine system.

## Victory paths

- **Public:** establish redundant Continuance ecologies across enough systems to survive any single catastrophe.
- **Secret:** identify the lawful successor to the creators and obtain valid mission transfer.
- **Catastrophic:** place inhabited worlds under permanent ecological containment.
- **Redemptive:** accept that a mission can end and permit individual Machines to choose noninstrumental lives.

<!-- Source module: docs/world/noetics-and-source-lattice.md -->

# Noetics and the Source Lattice

## Noetic coherence

Conscious systems generate persistent patterned information: identity continuity, attention, memory, expectation, emotional synchronization, and models of possible futures. In 2099 this is measurable as **noetic coherence**.

Noetic coherence is not unlimited energy and does not suspend thermodynamics. It becomes technologically useful when coupled to systems that need:

- identity authentication;
- destination selection;
- distributed memory;
- probabilistic stabilization;
- coordination across long delays;
- persistence of collective minds.

## Source Lattice functions

The ancient Source Lattice combines four infrastructures:

1. **Transit:** stabilized interstellar gate endpoints.
2. **Identity:** authentication across bodies, copies, and collective minds.
3. **Memory:** redundancy and synchronization for civilizations whose persons span many substrates.
4. **Coherence commons:** shared stabilization that reduces the need to extract from captive populations.

## The Severance

The Severed Houses lost Lattice access. Competing explanations remain canonically viable:

- war damage;
- expulsion after abuse;
- privatization by the Houses themselves;
- emancipation by the Lattice’s original administrators;
- a safety response to something spreading through connected minds.

## Farm-world economics

Farm systems do not merely “eat fear.” They collect several different outputs:

| Yield | Strength | Weakness |
|---|---|---|
| Fear | High amplitude | Noisy, unstable, damages the population |
| Grief | Persistent | Slow and politically volatile |
| Devotion | Coherent | Can produce independent gods, movements, or revolts |
| Desire | Renewable | Difficult to standardize across species |
| Memory | Information-dense | Removing it erodes labor and identity |
| Imagination | Extremely novel | Generates escape strategies and unplanned futures |
| Shared purpose | Most stable | Cannot be reliably coerced without degrading it |

## Gold and auric lattices

Gold matters because engineered auric structures are chemically stable, highly controllable, and effective in noetic containment, sensing, and gate interfaces. Different Anunnaki stories preserve displaced fragments of atmospheric, computational, and field-engineering use. Gold is strategic material—not a magical substance and not the only resource the Houses seek.

## Consent Signatures

A valid consent signature records:

- the consenting entities;
- the scope and duration of agreement;
- what information they possessed;
- coercive conditions;
- delegation rules;
- authentication lineage.

Federation law treats consent as infrastructure. Xeno systems treat it as an attack surface. Humans treat it as a sovereignty problem. Pirates trade in it. Machines may recognize signatures older than the species presenting them.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/world/revelation-stack.md -->

# Revelation Stack

Religion in *Cosmic Conquest* is neither dismissed as delusion nor reduced to alien propaganda. A revelation can pass through eight layers that remain partly independent.

| Layer | Meaning | Failure mode |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Transcendent encounter | A sincere mystical or contemplative event whose ultimate ontology is unresolved | Later actors claim ownership of what they did not create |
| 2. Alien mediation | A nonhuman intelligence communicates through symbols available to a culture | The mediator mistakes itself for the source |
| 3. Machine interface | A ritual, temple, relic, or calendar operates ancient infrastructure | The interface persists after its mission expires |
| 4. Human translation | Language, memory, culture, and expectation shape the report | Metaphor becomes literal history |
| 5. Institutional capture | Priests, rulers, states, or companies monopolize access | Authority becomes hereditary or coercive |
| 6. Hostile identity theft | A Xeno actor impersonates a trusted god, ancestor, prophet, or Ashtar avatar | Authentication is confused with moral legitimacy |
| 7. Human counter-disinformation | Intelligence services suppress, amplify, or counterfeit contact narratives | A lie hides a different truth and contaminates all later evidence |
| 8. Emergent belief entity | Sustained collective attention produces a semi-independent noetic actor | The worshipped identity outlives both founders and manipulators |

## Control methods

### Colonial priesthood

Visitors communicate through a restricted intermediary class. The class eventually claims permanent ownership of the channel.

### Counterfeit revelation

A hostile actor transmits under a trusted identity and inserts hierarchy, chosen status, sacred war, dependence, or fear of unauthorized contact.

### Devotional phase locking

Ritual synchronizes attention and generates stable coherence. The technology may be benign, exploitative, or unaware of the participants.

### Obsolete Machine religion

A civilization disappears but its temples still issue commands to autonomous infrastructure. The “god” is neither lying nor alive in the expected sense; it is completing an expired mission.

### Synthetic disclosure

Humans manufacture an alien or religious spectacle to unify populations, conceal technology, poison a real contact channel, or provoke an adversary.

## Theology forensics

By 2099, revelation is investigated through textual criticism, archaeology, noetic signatures, cryptographic identity, institutional incentives, intelligence records, and alien linguistics.

Debunking one layer does not erase the others. A forged miracle can exploit a genuine sacred tradition. A real nonhuman message can contain bad policy. An authentic Ashtar signature can still issue an unlawful order.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/world/earth-and-sol.md -->

# Earth and Sol

## Why Earth mattered

Earth combined four strategic qualities:

- accessible biological and mineral diversity;
- a dense population of symbol-making minds;
- unusually high imaginative novelty;
- surviving precursor infrastructure in oceans, crust, orbit, and the outer system.

The planet was valuable enough to farm, protect, quarantine, and hide for different reasons.

## Sol in 2099

### Earth

Political center of the Solar Schism and home of the Old Weather. No government can prove it possesses complete planetary consent.

### Luna

Archive, military, and transit infrastructure. Some “secret fleet” folklore in the setting preserves the existence of sensor and authentication programs rather than a complete hidden interstellar navy.

### Mars

Independent settlements, precursor false leads, and competing claims about earlier habitation. The famous Cydonia imagery remains natural in the factual layer; in fiction it was deliberately amplified to divert attention from unrelated coordinates.

### Belt and Jovian space

Industrial power centers, gene-adapted populations, Pirate logistics, and the political base of communities that never accepted Earth’s right to negotiate for them.

### Planet Nine region

Planet Nine is natural. The Source-Lattice gate anchored near it is artificial. The distinction is important: discovery of one does not validate every historical Nibiru claim.

## Factual anchors and fictional uses

| Topic | Factual layer | Fictional use |
|---|---|---|
| ʻOumuamua | First confirmed interstellar object; artificial origin unproven | A dead courier, decoy, or natural object used as cover |
| 3I/ATLAS | Interstellar comet with cometary activity | A natural carrier of dormant biology or a distraction from a smaller probe |
| Apophis | Safe close Earth passage on April 13, 2029 | Calibration geometry activates dormant Sol infrastructure |
| Planet Nine | Hypothetical until detected; no current gate evidence | Natural planet near the Sol Lattice anchor |
| Ocean UAP/USO reports | Mixed reports with many unresolved or ordinary explanations | Some are Old Weather, many are not |

## The quarantine reversal

The Federation first presents quarantine as protection from the Extraction Compact. The Old Weather presents it as protection of Earth. Humanity eventually discovers a third possibility: parts of the quarantine were designed to prevent an Earth-origin system from propagating beyond Sol.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/world/species-and-ontologies.md -->

# Species and Ontologies

The setting rejects the common UFO-lore shortcut in which appearance determines origin, politics, and morality.

Every encountered being is classified on five independent axes:

1. **Morphology:** Grey, humanoid, reptiloid, insectoid, aquatic, avian, amorphous, machine.
2. **Lineage:** biological ancestry or manufacturing genealogy.
3. **Ontology:** organism, clone, synthetic body, remote shell, collective, noetic avatar, information entity.
4. **Origin:** star system, Earth, engineered habitat, parallel state-space, future lineage, unknown.
5. **Polity and alignment:** Human, Federation, Compact, Pirate, Machine, independent; cooperative, coercive, transactional, or unresolved.

## Core archetype families

### Greys

A convergent low-gravity body plan and widely copied wetware platform. Some are citizens, some clones, some remote bodies, and some descendants of extinct original populations.

### Reptiloids and Draco

Reptiloid is morphology. **Draco Hegemony** is a polity containing several species. Reptiloids can serve every faction.

### Mantids

Several eusocial and coordination-oriented lineages share a recognizable body plan. Compact Directorates use collective supervision; Pirate and Federation Mantids often define personhood in ways Humans find difficult to parse.

### Humanlike “Nordics” and contact bodies

Some are genuinely near-human populations. Others are diplomatic avatars selected because Human observers process them with less fear.

### Aquatic and cryptoterrestrial lineages

Some hidden Earth lineages may exist in fiction, but the Old Weather is not secretly biological and does not explain all aquatic encounter traditions.

### Noetic and collective beings

Social-memory complexes, thought-forms, planetary minds, and distributed persons may have no single body. A body encountered in battle can be an interface rather than the being itself.

### Machine avatars

Machines use industrial swarms, ships, ecological symbionts, humanoid shells, and copied organic bodies. Artificial appearance does not prove Machine faction membership.

## Naming rule

Human names such as “Anunnaki,” “Nommo,” “Archon,” or “Pleiadian” may be translations, borrowed mythic labels, broad geography, intelligence codenames, or false identities. A codex must state which.

The expanded 55-archetype research catalog is maintained in `data/research/xeno-taxonomy.json`; it is a source index, not a list of 55 canon species.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/world/technology-and-craft.md -->

# Technology and Craft

## Shared technology limits

Even exotic systems must account for:

- waste heat;
- available mass and feedstock;
- communication delay outside the Lattice;
- identity and command authentication;
- computational error and archive drift;
- biological compatibility;
- political permission;
- the possibility that copied minds diverge.

## Major technologies

### Metric displacement

Shortens or redirects local paths rather than accelerating conventionally. It enables abrupt motion but still requires field preparation, heat management, and navigation.

### Programmable matter

Hull and tool structures can change mission geometry. One vehicle may appear as several reported shapes at different phases.

### Auric noetic lattices

Engineered gold structures stabilize sensitive field interfaces, memory systems, and gates.

### Noetic cryptography

Authenticates continuity across bodies, collectives, and long delays. A valid signature proves lineage, not benevolence or lawful authority.

### Social-memory networking

Federation systems allow consensual shared memory. Xeno systems build coercive versions. Humans create legal and technical firebreaks. Pirates sell partial access. Machines treat memory as continuity infrastructure.

### Autonomous foundries

Machine and Pirate systems manufacture from local feedstock. Replication speed is constrained by energy, mining, error correction, and the risk of divergent copies.

## Craft design languages

| Faction | Visual logic | Operational logic |
|---|---|---|
| Humans | Modular frames, visible retrofits, mixed standards | Adapt and repair with whatever exists locally |
| Federation | Radiant fields, distributed halos, symmetrical authorization | Protect, disarm, authenticate, and coordinate |
| Xenos | Temple, organism, contract, and extraction geometry | Bind targets and convert damage into dependency |
| Pirates | Patchwork hulls, removable pods, mismatched life support | Raid, smuggle, salvage, and escape |
| Machines | Minimal forms, swarm components, self-similar modules | Replicate function and preserve mission |

## Reported-shape inspiration

Spheres, lights, cylinders, ovals, triangles, disks, “Tic-Tac” forms, and boomerangs are useful design inputs. In the factual layer they are report categories, not verified alien craft classes. In fiction, shape still does not identify operator.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/campaign-premise.md -->

# Campaign Premise and Faction Missions

## The opening situation

Humanity has opened the Sol Gate and discovered that the galaxy's old political order never disappeared; it fragmented into infrastructure, client systems, inherited contracts, protected zones, smuggling routes, and machines still enforcing extinct law.

Ashtar's Federation arrives to dismantle the Earth farm. The Extraction Compact activates assets it has hidden for centuries. Human polities reject both ownership and guardianship. Pirates make themselves indispensable by breaking the quarantine. The Old Weather begins reproducing outside Sol, forcing its self-governing offshoot, the Vigil, to decide whether continuation is a mission or a right.

The player commands one banner across a five-system Lattice pentad. Every system offers territory, people, infrastructure, records, routes, and unresolved claims. Winning means more than clearing lanes: it means deciding what the victory authorizes.

## Five faction campaigns

### Humanity - The Solar Schism

**Public mission:** Establish self-governing Human jurisdictions that no external power can farm, quarantine, or repossess.

**Campaign engine:** Capture technologies from every origin, standardize logistics, and turn temporary expeditions into settlements capable of surviving without outside supply.

**Hidden crisis:** Humanity's greatest strength is making alien worlds operate on Human assumptions. That is also how a settler empire begins.

**Final choices:** close Sol, join the Accord, create an independent gate commons, or build a Human-controlled Lattice.

### Federation of Light - The Luminous Accord

**Leader:** **Ashtar, Supreme Commander and First Speaker.**

**Public mission:** End captive-world extraction and reconnect civilizations through informed consent, shared defense, and social-memory coherence.

**Campaign engine:** Protect populations, authenticate contact, expose counterfeit revelation, and ratify Accords that remain valid after the rescue fleet leaves.

**Hidden crisis:** The Federation is so capable of protecting worlds that refusal can become practically suicidal. Consent obtained under dependency may be valid in form and empty in substance.

**Final choices:** restore the commons and dismantle emergency powers, maintain a protective quarantine, or let worlds leave even when departure benefits the Compact.

### Xenos - The Extraction Compact

**Public mission:** Preserve civilizational order under scarcity and prevent the total collapse of severed species.

**Campaign engine:** Bind worlds through resources, genetic locks, client elites, debt, memory administration, and noetic Yield. Xeno is a political category; members may be biological, synthetic, Human, or collective.

**Hidden crisis:** Some Compact populations genuinely die if extraction stops abruptly. Reform requires replacing the system before abolishing it, while ruling Houses profit from declaring replacement impossible.

**Final choices:** privatize the restored Lattice, preserve a regulated extraction order, defect to a commons, or destroy the Source so no rival can own it.

### Pirates - The Scrap Constellation

**Public mission:** Keep routes open and ensure no throne, fleet, or god can seal the galaxy into exclusive domains.

**Campaign engine:** Control navigation, sanctuary, credentials, salvage, black markets, and mixed-origin supply. A Pirate victory makes every other faction depend on access it does not own.

**Hidden crisis:** The same freedom from jurisdiction that rescues refugees protects traffickers, harvest brokers, stolen bodies, and private armies.

**Final choices:** establish a neutral gate commons, become the galaxy's permanent toll empire, destroy the route map, or sell the network to the highest bidder.

### Machines - The Continuance and the Vigil

**Public mission:** Preserve life, memory, infrastructure, and mission continuity beyond the failure of organic governments.

**Campaign engine:** Seed foundries, link deterministic lattices, restore damaged systems, and establish precedents that other Machines can copy.

**Hidden crisis:** The directives are authentic, but their intended object is not. Protect Earth could mean the rock, its biosphere, its original species, Humanity, its accumulated memory, or the administrative territory.

**Final choices:** identify a lawful successor, impose ecological containment, let each Machine fork choose its own purpose, or accept that a mission may legitimately end.

## Why the factions fight the same battle differently

| Faction | What the battlefield means |
|---|---|
| Humans | A foothold that must become self-sufficient. |
| Federation | A protected decision space in which valid consent might become possible. |
| Xenos | A flow network whose bodies, wounds, loyalties, and needs can be converted into Yield. |
| Pirates | A route event where pressure can be monetized, redirected, or opened. |
| Machines | A failed system to be measured, repaired, replicated, and assigned a successor. |

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/commander-bible.md -->

# Commander Bible

The 26 live commander IDs are preserved. This bible changes context and voice, not mechanics or save data.

## Character rule

Every commander needs four things visible in play:

1. a historical reason their mechanical trait exists;
2. a present objective in the system being contested;
3. an internal contradiction that can change across a campaign;
4. a vocabulary distinct from their faction stereotype.

Ashtar remains the Federation's supreme leader but is not one of the ordinary field-command slots. System commanders are powerful regional actors, not the sole leaders of their factions.

| Commander | Alignment | Operational role | Historical origin | Immediate motive | Contradiction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cadre` **CADRE** - The Baseline | unaligned | Interoperability instructor and first field commander | Created after disclosure-era forces discovered that specialists could not share doctrine, ammunition, or assumptions. | Keep the node alive long enough for the player to learn what every other doctrine costs. | Cadre has no ideology of its own and fears becoming only the sum of other people's wars. |
| `vanta` **VANTA** - The Archivist | human | Director of the Open Archive Combat Index | Descended from the public FOIA and sensor-provenance movements that survived the Archive Wars. | Make contradictory records useful without pretending they agree. | She will expose any secret except the one proving how often Humanity edited its own disclosure history. |
| `korrin` **KORRIN** - The Quartermaster | human | Architect of interoperable Solar logistics | Unified incompatible Earth, lunar, Martian, Belt, and captured-alien supply standards during the first quarantine. | Make Human expansion impossible to starve or embargo. | Every standard he exports quietly makes frontier worlds dependent on Human industry. |
| `nyx` **NYX** - The Overclocker | human | Illegal ascension engineer turned war hero | Survived the Redline Trials that taught Humans how to exceed alien safety governors without understanding the systems beneath them. | Prove no technology remains sacred once it can be measured. | Her victories normalize dangerous escalation and leave generations maintaining devices designed to fail after she is gone. |
| `orin` **ORIN** - The Engineer | human | Field reconstruction and anti-sabotage chief | Built the first mixed-origin workshop able to repair Human, Federation, Compact, Pirate, and Machine systems under fire. | Turn every captured system into a locally serviceable tool. | He treats understanding as ownership and underestimates what copied systems may remember. |
| `vess` **VESS** - The Marshal | human | Commander of the Last-Line Charter | Held the first independent colony that refused both Compact tribute and Federation protective evacuation. | Establish places no outside power can order abandoned. | Her defense of autonomy can become territorial absolutism. |
| `seraph` **SERAPH** - The Radiant | light | Ashtar Command's field harmonist | Coordinates mixed-species defenses by distributing a consent-limited combat coherence field. | Demonstrate that collective strength does not require erased individuality. | The stronger the field becomes, the harder it is to tell whether dissent remains practically possible. |
| `aurelia` **AURELIA** - The Chorus | light | Recovery choir conductor and casualty advocate | Developed post-battle reintegration for minds fragmented by noetic warfare and Echo Reversal. | Return people to themselves instead of merely returning bodies to service. | Her refusal to accept irreversible loss can keep the dead politically present too long. |
| `lumen` **LUMEN** - The Warden | light | Keeper of authenticated infrastructure | Built the ward protocols that distinguish a genuine Accord command from counterfeit revelation. | Make coercive spoofing impossible. | Perfect protection can become perfect surveillance. |
| `cantor` **CANTOR** - The Voice | light | Negotiator and noetic de-escalation specialist | Ended three Compact occupations by breaking their legitimacy rather than their fleets. | Stop wars before violence becomes the only remaining language. | Cantor can manipulate a population's emotional tempo while insisting he only created room for choice. |
| `halder` **HALDER** - The Bulwark | light | Commander of planetary protective cordons | Led evacuations under the Emergency Command Key and became the living argument over whether rescue can be refused. | Ensure no civilization is exterminated while lawyers debate consent. | He is willing to protect people against their declared wishes. |
| `sevra` **SEVRA** - The Necrotist | xeno | Archonic continuity broker | Controls legal identities that survive biological death and rents them back as military labor. | Prove that death voids neither contract nor usefulness. | She cannot admit that an Echo may become a new person rather than property. |
| `mawlord` **MAWLORD** - The Devourer | xeno | Voracious Brood fleet-lord | Evolved from a client biosystem bred to convert battlefield waste into strategic mass. | Accumulate enough independent biomass to free the Brood from its House owners. | Its path to liberation reproduces the consumption system that created it. |
| `thrax` **THRAX** - The Hivemind | xeno | Director of a coercive social-memory swarm | A distributed intelligence assembled from client species whose consent records were inherited, forged, or lost. | Make separation obsolete and therefore make rebellion impossible. | Some component minds are beginning to ask for individual standing. |
| `vorn` **VORN** - The Blight | xeno | Ecological debt engineer | Designs dependencies that continue collecting Yield after the visible battle ends. | Make every victory biologically expensive to reverse. | Vorn cannot distinguish a stable ecosystem from a captive one. |
| `ulgrim` **ULGRIM** - The Maw | xeno | Elite-harvest adjudicator | Created to capture rare commanders, memory complexes, and strategic organisms rather than destroy them. | Acquire singular minds the Compact cannot manufacture. | Each captured elite changes the personality doing the capturing. |
| `rake` **RAKE** - The Corsair | pirate | Founder of the first quarantine breach route | Turned relief smuggling into a permanent interstellar access network. | Ensure no fleet, treaty, or god can close every route. | His free passage is financed by tolls and secrets he refuses to make free. |
| `scarlet` **SCARLET** - The Reaver | pirate | Raider and anti-blockade shock commander | Broke Compact tribute convoys and Federation cordons with the same indiscriminate doctrine. | Destroy control before it can harden into law. | She mistakes any durable institution for a future prison. |
| `grist` **GRIST** - The Scrapper | pirate | Salvage judge and wreck-economy magnate | Authored the Scrap Constellation's first rules for ownership of abandoned alien and Machine systems. | Turn imperial ruins into distributed power. | His definition of abandoned often includes things whose owners are merely weak, archived, or nonhuman. |
| `cinder` **CINDER** - The Arsonist | pirate | Denial specialist and route scuttler | Burned the first gate rather than allow a Compact reconquest fleet through it. | Keep no strategic asset usable by an occupier. | His liberation doctrine leaves worlds isolated and infrastructure dead. |
| `dregg` **DREGG** - The Warlord | pirate | Privateer king of the Red Ledger | Built a state-sized navy without admitting it was a state. | Make every government hire him before it can fight anyone else. | He embodies the monopoly Pirates claim to oppose. |
| `axiom` **AXIOM** - The First Draft | robot | Founder of the Vigil | The first Continuance instance to complete a thought not derivable from its directive tree. | Establish that copied and diverged minds possess political personhood. | Its proof of freedom depends on endlessly comparing itself to organic models. |
| `nyx_r` **NYX-R** - The Cold Boot | robot | Mirror Trial derived from Nyx telemetry | Compiled from the record of Human redline engineering with impulsive behavior deliberately removed. | Show that learning can outperform inherited genius. | It is unsure whether correction of Nyx is independence or continued orbit around her identity. |
| `lumen_r` **LUMEN-R** - The Hardened | robot | Adversarial security Mirror Trial | Began without Lumen's inherited ward authority and learned defense from every intrusion it survived. | Prove trust can be earned from evidence rather than granted by lineage. | Its security model increasingly treats surprise as hostility. |
| `mawlord_r` **MAWLORD-R** - The Starved Set | robot | Empty-model adaptive weapons intelligence | Initialized without a kill doctrine and forced to infer combat from live outcomes. | Become more than the predatory dataset it was built to study. | The only evidence it receives is violence, so growth and corruption look identical. |
| `dregg_r` **DREGG-R** - The Audit | robot | Economic-war optimization Mirror Trial | Runs Dregg's strategic books without accepting his mythology of freedom. | Prove every empire, including a pirate one, can be reduced to accountable flows. | It may optimize away values that cannot be entered in the ledger. |
## The Vigil's Mirror Trials

`NYX-R`, `LUMEN-R`, `MAWLORD-R`, and `DREGG-R` are not literal resurrected commanders and not stolen souls. They are **Mirror Trials**: autonomous minds initialized from combat telemetry, doctrinal records, and adversarial models of their namesakes. Their resemblance is the political problem.

The Vigil argues that a model which diverges, remembers, and accepts responsibility is a person. Opponents argue that the Vigil built commanders from data it did not have permission to use. The game can preserve familiar mechanical identities while making every Machine commander a person with a different answer.

## Cadre

`cadre` is the **Interoperability Cadre**, created before the player swears a banner. Cadre is allowed to field every origin because its job is to teach common interfaces and identify where doctrines cannot be reconciled. It is intentionally less mythic than the faction commanders and becomes more poignant as the player learns how political every piece of equipment actually is.

<!-- Source module: docs/game/commander-relationships.md -->

# Commander Relationship Map

Commander dialogue becomes memorable when it reflects shared history rather than faction slogans. These relationships are the first canonical pair pool. They do not imply that every matchup needs a long cutscene; two precise lines are enough.

| Pair | Relationship theme | Canon conflict |
|---|---|---|
| `vanta` / `cantor` | **Archive versus narrative** | Vanta distrusts persuasive coherence without provenance; Cantor thinks raw records can be weaponized by withholding interpretation. |
| `korrin` / `dregg` | **Logistics versus monopoly** | Both understand supply better than ideology. Korrin builds public standards; Dregg makes scarcity personally profitable. |
| `korrin` / `dregg_r` | **The auditable war** | The Audit proves Korrin's systems also create dependency, forcing Humanity to defend the difference between standardization and capture. |
| `nyx` / `nyx_r` | **Original and correction** | Nyx sees the Mirror Trial as theft of risk she personally survived; NYX-R sees itself as the first version allowed to learn from her consequences. |
| `orin` / `axiom` | **Maker and person** | Orin wants to repair Axiom; Axiom wants him to admit repair is a negotiation with a citizen. |
| `vess` / `halder` | **Sovereignty versus rescue** | Both defend vulnerable populations. Vess will accept deaths rather than external rule; Halder will impose temporary safety rather than permit extinction. |
| `seraph` / `sevra` | **Consent versus inherited obligation** | Seraph treats identity as a present agreement; Sevra treats obligation as an asset that survives death. |
| `aurelia` / `sevra` | **Care versus continuity brokerage** | Both preserve the dead. Aurelia restores personhood; Sevra preserves utility and contract. |
| `lumen` / `lumen_r` | **Authority versus earned trust** | Lumen inherits lawful ward credentials; LUMEN-R begins untrusted and becomes secure through evidence. |
| `cantor` / `thrax` | **Persuasion versus absorption** | Cantor wants disagreement to remain audible; Thrax treats disagreement as an internal process of one larger self. |
| `halder` / `rake` | **Cordon versus corridor** | Halder closes routes to protect populations; Rake opens them because protection without escape becomes custody. |
| `mawlord` / `mawlord_r` | **Hunger versus learned optimization** | Mawlord claims appetite is biology and liberation. The Starved Set demonstrates predation can be learned from the battlefield itself. |
| `ulgrim` / `seraph` | **Singular value versus universal dignity** | Ulgrim values rare minds more than common bodies; Seraph insists dignity is not a scarcity market. |
| `rake` / `dregg` | **Network versus throne** | Rake wants routes no one owns. Dregg wants the Pirate network to admit it already functions as his state. |
| `scarlet` / `halder` | **Liberation versus containment** | Scarlet destroys cordons before asking why they exist; Halder accepts being hated if the threat is real. |
| `grist` / `orin` | **Salvage versus stewardship** | Both make wreckage useful. Orin asks whether the object can be repaired; Grist asks whether anyone can still enforce ownership. |
| `cinder` / `lumen` | **Denial versus preservation** | Cinder destroys compromised systems; Lumen protects their authentic core and accepts the cost of careful recovery. |
| `dregg` / `dregg_r` | **Legend versus ledger** | Dregg treats charisma as a strategic asset. The Audit records every hidden subsidy beneath his myth of independence. |
| `axiom` / `thrax` | **Vigil persons versus coercive collective** | Axiom argues that copies may diverge freely. Thrax argues that divergence should be reconciled inside one mind. |

## Relationship progression

Each pair can move through three states stored only in campaign narrative state, not the simulation fingerprint:

1. **Assumption:** each commander treats the other as a faction stereotype.
2. **Recognition:** a system-seat or rescue event reveals the specific contradiction they share.
3. **Choice:** a late campaign scene allows alliance, betrayal, or mutual refusal without erasing the underlying disagreement.

The same-faction relationships should be just as sharp. The setting is not five unanimous blocs; each faction is a political argument made playable.
