# Faction Troops and the Vigil

## Faction troops

These 25 stable IDs are the people, vehicles, organisms, and bodies recovered at the second-star threshold. Rescue does not always mean ownership: the exact relationship is recruitment, asylum, contract, emancipation, copying, or mission transfer depending on the unit.

| Unit | Origin | Formation | Why it exists | What it says about the faction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `trooper` **Trooper** | human | Solar Line Corps infantry | Standardized modular infantry adapted to mixed gravity and alien atmospheres. | Holds ground and proves Human conquest is institutional, not merely technological. |
| `gunskiff` **Gunskiff** | human | Belt-built close support vehicle | A pressure-rated mining tug converted into a fast weapons platform. | Mobility and field improvisation. |
| `linebreaker` **Linebreaker** | human | Breach armor detachment | Descended from habitat rescue rigs designed to move rubble while under decompression. | Turns survival engineering into assault doctrine. |
| `dragoon` **Dragoon** | human | Rapid orbital cavalry | Drop infantry whose legal charter lets them establish temporary jurisdiction before diplomats arrive. | Human settlement can become occupation in a single landing. |
| `vanguard` **Vanguard** | human | Command standard and discipline aura | Carries a portable civil registry as well as a battlefield standard. | A Human banner is law, logistics, and culture arriving together. |
| `votary` **Votary** | light | Consent-certified line defender | A signatory who accepted one precisely scoped defensive duty. | The ward is proof of authenticated participation, not spiritual worth. |
| `censer` **Censer** | light | Recovery and coherence medic | Projects a restorative field through shared-memory triage. | Keeps formations alive without erasing individual pain. |
| `sanctifier` **Sanctifier** | light | Heavy ward carrier | Maintains a protected consensus volume against corruption and spoofing. | Defense can also become exclusion. |
| `oriflamme` **Oriflamme** | light | Accord standard bearer | Broadcasts an authenticated treaty state that reinforces nearby allies. | The battlefield literally changes around valid consent. |
| `luminark` **Luminark** | light | Mobile cathedral-engine | A large shield and memory vessel preserving a whole units witness chain. | A fortress made from collective remembrance. |
| `chitling` **Chitling** | xeno | Voracious Brood skirmisher | A small client organism grown to consume battlefield waste. | The Compact deploys dependency as biology. |
| `gnawling` **Gnawling** | xeno | Voracious Brood assault caste | Bred to metabolize armor and pass usable matter back through the brood. | Every wound becomes supply. |
| `bloatpod` **Bloatpod** | xeno | Mobile nutrient and spore vessel | Stores mass, toxins, and replacement bodies for a local brood. | The unit is logistics disguised as flesh. |
| `hivelord` **Hivelord** | xeno | Coercive coordination node | Maintains a command field across genetically incompatible client organisms. | A political hierarchy implemented as a nervous system. |
| `broodmother` **Broodmother** | xeno | Strategic biomass carrier | Produces, repairs, and legally registers new client bodies. | Its offspring are both soldiers and collateral under Compact law. |
| `cutter` **Cutter** | pirate | Fast boarding craft | A route-runner stripped to thrust, hooks, and a gun. | Access first, survivability second. |
| `boarder` **Boarder** | pirate | Mixed-species breach crew | Every member brings a different atmosphere kit, weapon standard, and reason for exile. | Pirate diversity is operational, not ceremonial. |
| `scrapjack` **Scrapjack** | pirate | Combat salvager | Pulls useful systems from wrecks before the fight has ended. | The border between rescue and theft is one moving body. |
| `wrecker` **Wrecker** | pirate | Scuttle and demolition chassis | Built to make ports, gates, and ships unusable to the next owner. | Pirates can preserve freedom by destroying common infrastructure. |
| `ironhulk` **Ironhulk** | pirate | Many-hull siege vessel | No two subsystems share an origin, species, or warranty. | The Scrap Constellation survives because nothing can embargo every part. |
| `stitch` **Stitch** | robot | Repair and relay microframe | Joins damaged systems and leaves a forward synchronization node. | Machine conquest begins as maintenance. |
| `fabricant` **Fabricant** | robot | General-purpose production body | Prints replacement components while recording every deviation as precedent. | Manufacturing becomes law by repetition. |
| `splicer` **Splicer** | robot | Interface and protocol conversion frame | Makes incompatible organic and synthetic systems temporarily interoperable. | Translation can become quiet control. |
| `gantry` **Gantry** | robot | Mobile industrial support chassis | Carries tools, ammunition, and construction logic rather than a singular weapon. | The battlefield is treated as unfinished infrastructure. |
| `omniframe` **Omniframe** | robot | Consensus anchor and heavy platform | Coordinates nearby Machine instances without making them one mind. | The Vigil defines unity as negotiated synchronization. |
## Doctrinal mechanics

- **Human Salvage Armor:** Human detachments turn battlefield wreckage into standardized protection. This is adaptation with a colonial shadow: everything becomes a compatible resource.
- **Federation Vow:** A shield can pass between signatories because the ward authenticates a shared defensive obligation. The Vow is scoped consent, not surrender of a soul.
- **Xeno Mass:** The Voracious Brood converts nearby death into growth. These five units are one Compact client-army, not the biology of every Xeno civilization.
- **Pirate Scuttle:** Pirate bodies and vehicles are designed to deny useful capture. Their deaths damage nearby infrastructure because autonomy includes deciding what survives you.
- **Machine Relay:** Vigil units leave forward nodes because every action is also infrastructure for the next instance.

## The Vigil is not a faction army

The neutral enemy roster is a family of legacy Old Weather chassis. Their shapes describe maintenance and enforcement functions that war has turned hostile.

| Vigil family | Current examples | Original mission | Battlefield behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Survey and pursuit** | Picket/Crawler, Outrunner/Sprinter, Mote/Mite, Lantern/Wisp | Inspect boundaries, carry readings, pursue unsafe traffic. | Baseline bodies, speed, flight, and early pressure. |
| **Containment** | Palisade/Bulwark, Aegis Sentinel, Ironmarch/Juggernaut | Hold hazardous material and survive environmental failure. | Armor, shielding, and heavy lives-cost. |
| **Service ecology** | Restorer/Mender, Divider/Cluster, Fragment/Spawnling, Foundry Carrier | Repair damaged nodes, divide workloads, and deliver replacement frames. | Healing, splitting, summoning, and reinforcement. |
| **Access control** | Interdictor/Jammer, Phase Courier/Blink, Ghost Chassis/Wraith, Nullifier | Deny unauthorized systems, pass sealed boundaries, or suppress unsafe functions. | Jamming, teleportation, phasing, and ability shutdown. |
| **Continuity enforcement** | Reconstructor/Revenant, Warden | Restore approved state and reject unauthorized displacement. | Revival and control resistance. |
| **Command pylons** | Marshal, Anchor, Cadence, Bastion | Publish local mission priorities to subordinate machines. | Auras for frenzy, anchoring, haste, and armor. |
| **Catastrophe frames** | Colossus, Vesper, Myriad, Oracle, Reclaimer, Harbinger | Execute emergency protocols too large for ordinary jurisdiction. | Miniboss and boss encounters with rules that cannot be solved by simple control effects. |

An enemy dossier should always state both the **current tactical behavior** and the **lost civilian or custodial purpose**. This keeps the Vigil tragic and alien without making it another evil robot swarm.


## Exact Vigil chassis coverage

The live enemy count is explained as **29 neutral Vigil chassis plus 25 faction-unit definitions = 54 enemy definitions**. The table below supplies a civil or custodial origin for each neutral chassis. Exact hit points, resistances, abilities, and boss flags remain code-authoritative.

| Lore registry ID | Family | Original function | Canon meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `shardling` **Shard Sentry** | containment | Faceted inspection and debris-containment chassis | Splash resistance reflects armor designed to scatter industrial shock. |
| `basalt` **Basalt Marcher** | containment | Geological stabilization frame sintered from local stone | Slow immunity reflects a mass-moving machine built to proceed through disasters. |
| `crawler` **Picket** | survey | Routine perimeter and standing-guard chassis | The baseline Vigil body, issued wherever a jurisdiction still recognizes a boundary. |
| `sprinter` **Outrunner** | survey | High-speed inspection and message frame | Stealth and speed reflect a scout stripped of everything not needed to reach the next checkpoint. |
| `mite` **Mote** | service | Microscale repair and sensor fragment | Numbers replace durability because the original task assumed disposable swarms. |
| `bulwark` **Palisade** | containment | Mobile hazard wall and pressure barrier | Armor and resistance come from civil containment rather than military nobility. |
| `wisp` **Lantern** | survey | Aerial illumination, atmospheric sensing, and route-marking unit | Flight makes it a living signal light that ignores ground geometry. |
| `aegis` **Aegis Sentinel** | containment | Shielded custody and protected-transfer chassis | Its ward once moved fragile archives and organisms through unsafe areas. |
| `mender` **Restorer** | service | Field repair, triage, and continuity maintenance unit | Healing is its original purpose, not a combat spell. |
| `cluster` **Divider** | service | Workload-distribution chassis that splits into specialized fragments | Division turns one broad maintenance task into several local bodies. |
| `spawnling` **Fragment** | service | A child task-instance shed by a larger Divider or Carrier | It inherits one instruction and almost no context. |
| `juggernaut` **Ironmarch** | containment | Heavy transport and catastrophe-clearance platform | High lives-cost reflects a frame designed to cross a whole jurisdiction intact. |
| `jammer` **Interdictor** | access | Safety transmitter that denies unauthorized remote control | Jamming is a permissions response, not electronic malice. |
| `blink` **Phase Courier** | access | Sealed dispatch unit using discontinuous Lattice routing | Teleportation is a transit privilege preserved after the institution that granted it vanished. |
| `warden` **Warden** | continuity | Senior jurisdiction guardian with control-resistant authority | It was built to keep operating when local systems failed or were compromised. |
| `wraith` **Ghost Chassis** | access | Inspection body that alternates between material and noetic state | Phasing lets it cross sealed industrial volumes without opening them. |
| `nullifier` **Nullifier** | access | Emergency diagnostic frame that suppresses nonessential functions | It reduces systems to minimum safe behavior so faults can be isolated. |
| `revenant` **Reconstructor** | continuity | Approved-state recovery chassis | Revival restores the last valid record, raising the question of whether the returned instance is the same person. |
| `carrier` **Foundry Carrier** | service | Mobile fabrication and replacement-body transport | Summoning is manufacturing performed under a still-valid emergency order. |
| `warchief` **Marshal Pylon** | command | Publishes aggressive emergency priority to nearby frames | Its frenzy aura is a civil disaster mode repurposed by war. |
| `anchorite` **Anchor Pylon** | command | Stabilizes position, mass, and routing around critical work | Displacement resistance is literally its reason to exist. |
| `herald` **Cadence Pylon** | command | Synchronizes movement and work cycles across a jurisdiction | Haste is an inherited scheduling command. |
| `bastion` **Bastion Pylon** | command | Distributes structural reinforcement to subordinate frames | Armor aura once kept repair teams alive inside collapsing infrastructure. |
| `mb_colossus` **COLOSSUS** | catastrophe | Massive structural-rescue and planetary-defense frame | A miniboss because its emergency authority overrides ordinary control. |
| `mb_seraph` **VESPER** | catastrophe | Low-observability night and sensor-denial response frame | Its identity should center on uncertainty, concealment, and denied targeting. |
| `mb_hydra` **MYRIAD** | catastrophe | Distributed replication emergency with many valid bodies | Its threat is not one strong unit but the jurisdiction treating multiplicity as continuity. |
| `mb_oracle` **ORACLE** | catastrophe | Predictive hazard-management intelligence | It acts on expected future damage, creating the appearance of foreknowledge. |
| `mb_lich` **RECLAIMER** | catastrophe | Emergency salvage and mission-recovery engine | It treats destroyed systems as material that has not yet been reassigned. |
| `harbinger` **HARBINGER** | catastrophe | Supreme extinction-level enforcement frame | The boss is a warning protocol made physical: if it appears, the jurisdiction believes ordinary governance has failed. |

The elite display name **RECLAIMER** uses the exact live ID `mb_lich`; the Human tower uses `reclaimer`. Writers and UI code must resolve this by ID rather than display name. The other catastrophe frames similarly retain their live `mb_*` IDs even when those IDs reflect older internal names.

<!-- Source module: docs/game/tower-bible.md -->

# Tower Arsenal Bible

All 60 current tower IDs and mechanics remain intact. **Origin is an engineering doctrine, not a racial lock.** A player can acquire a foreign tower through treaty, capture, rescue, salvage, licensing, reverse engineering, or Machine precedent.

The display copy should answer three questions in this order:

1. What does this tower do on the board?
2. Who originally built the doctrine and for what non-game reason?
3. What moral or political consequence follows from using it?

## Human Adaptation Arsenal

| Stable ID | Tower | Historical origin | Why the mechanic belongs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bolt` | **BOLT** | Solar militia standard, designed after the first off-world logistics split. | Cheap reliability and kill-reload represent industrial standardization. |
| `cryo` | **CRYO** | A lunar volatile-control projector repurposed to arrest movement. | Humans weaponize environmental engineering. |
| `mortar` | **MORTAR** | Habitat-defense artillery that fires from allied sensor tracks. | It depends on the broader Human observation network. |
| `arc` | **ARC** | A captured Lattice capacitor grounded through a Human mast. | Reverse engineering turns alien field behavior into lane geometry. |
| `flak` | **FLAK** | Anti-transmedium interception gun created after conventional targeting repeatedly lost low-signature craft. | It forces aerial threats into the shared battlespace. |
| `canister` | **CANISTER** | Mining and hull-stripping aerosols adapted for armored targets. | A utilitarian Human tool with serious ethical restrictions outside combat zones. |
| `reclaimer` | **RECLAIMER** | Compact void hardware recovered from a wreck and mounted without full theoretical understanding. | Humanity gains strength by domesticating what it does not yet comprehend. |
| `quartermaster` | **QUARTERMASTER** | A deployable requisition office descended from Solar emergency logistics. | Paperwork becomes a combat multiplier. |
| `rampart` | **RAMPART** | Prefabricated habitat shielding converted into lane barricades. | Settlement technology literally becomes defense. |
| `arbalest` | **ARBALEST** | A patient magnetic siege system that stores energy while it waits for a legal firing solution. | Human doctrine rewards preparation over exotic automation. |
| `bombard` | **BOMBARD** | A compact lunar mass-driver battery. | The largest Human gun is powerful because the coalition can feed and spot it. |
| `coldfront` | **COLDFRONT** | A climate-control field built from colony weather infrastructure. | Humans manufacture local conditions rather than merely endure them. |

## Federation Accord Arsenal

| Stable ID | Tower | Historical origin | Why the mechanic belongs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `beacon` | **BEACON** | An Accord coherence allocator that chooses one protected asset at a time. | Federation power concentrates through authenticated priority rather than mass fire. |
| `prism` | **PRISM** | A continuous noetic beam that becomes stronger while identity lock remains valid. | Trust and focus are mechanically inseparable. |
| `chrono` | **CHRONO** | A local memory-state restoration device, not literal universal time travel. | It reasserts a recently authenticated position. |
| `shepherd` | **SHEPHERD** | An Echo Reversal escort that stabilizes reconstructed allies. | The Federation treats echoes as protected continuations, not ammunition. |
| `ward` | **WARD** | Anti-spoof sanctity field created after counterfeit Ashtar transmissions. | Authentication is the Federation's most important armor. |
| `custodian` | **CUSTODIAN** | Consent-scoped wardens assigned to absorb a specific breach. | Sacrifice is lawful only inside the scope each warden signed. |
| `concord` | **CONCORD** | A harmonic chain that leaves targets mutually legible to the rest of the line. | The Federation wins by making isolated attacks cooperative. |
| `sepulchre` | **SEPULCHRE** | A temporary continuity archive for destroyed emplacements. | Loss is remembered long enough to keep serving, then deliberately released. |
| `orison` | **ORISON** | A battlefield dedication protocol that names one threat as the shared obligation. | Collective purpose grants strength but risks sanctifying violence. |
| `antiphon` | **ANTIPHON** | A response array that turns allied detachment losses into defensive volleys. | The Accord answers sacrifice rather than consuming it silently. |
| `monstrance` | **MONSTRANCE** | A revelation beam exposing armor, resistances, and hidden protections. | It makes one target radically knowable. |
| `pharos` | **PHAROS** | A rotating approach-light descended from evacuation beacons. | Its unalterable sweep represents law applied without favoritism. |

## Compact Extraction Arsenal

| Stable ID | Tower | Historical origin | Why the mechanic belongs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `toxin` | **TOXIN** | A House biochemistry that attacks current vitality rather than armor. | Extraction is most efficient before a subject is exhausted. |
| `siphon` | **SIPHON** | A noetic recovery plant that converts inflicted damage into command continuity. | Violence literally funds the occupier's survival. |
| `executioner` | **EXECUTIONER** | A client-control weapon for culling assets below a profitability threshold. | The Compact formalizes mercy and disposal as the same calculation. |
| `siren` | **SIREN** | An Archonic command lure that rewrites a target's immediate allegiance. | Consent is replaced by a technically valid but manufactured choice state. |
| `alchemist` | **ALCHEMIST** | A death-transmutation platform that permanently learns from nearby kills. | Every casualty becomes proprietary research. |
| `reckoning` | **RECKONING** | A damage-ledger weapon that bills a target for recent harm. | The Compact turns collective violence into debt. |
| `ichor` | **ICHOR** | A living digestive spray optimized for already wounded bodies. | The deeper the wound, the more efficiently Yield is collected. |
| `gestalt` | **GESTALT** | A local coercive group-mind that folds nearby deaths into itself. | Growth is powerful, permanent, and vulnerable to memory collapse. |
| `maw` | **MAW** | A removal organism that consumes one strategic body outside the Echo system. | The Compact can deny even an enemy's right to leave a record. |
| `veil` | **HUNGERING VEIL** | An Archonic field that records healing and returns it as liability. | Care itself becomes taxable. |
| `suture` | **SUTURE** | A graft system that distributes wounds across linked subjects. | Individuals become one balance sheet without becoming one consenting mind. |
| `impaler` | **IMPALER** | A board-wide execution spine that seeks the deepest existing wound. | The Compact attacks weakness wherever the wider system creates it. |

## Scrap Constellation Arsenal

| Stable ID | Tower | Historical origin | Why the mechanic belongs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pyre` | **PYRE** | A salvaged fuel tank, igniter, and absent safety governor. | Pirate strength and self-destruction share one heat meter. |
| `tether` | **TETHER** | A cargo harpoon and salvage winch turned displacement weapon. | Pirates force the enemy to travel the same dangerous route twice. |
| `sapper` | **SAPPER** | Route-denial mines planted during quiet intervals. | Pirates turn unused time and unclaimed ground into hidden leverage. |
| `saboteur` | **SABOTEUR** | A cross-board intrusion cell descended from gate credential theft. | It attacks an opponent's infrastructure rather than the neutral wave. |
| `glaive` | **GLAIVE** | A lane-following cutter derived from rotating salvage tools. | The path itself becomes a weapon. |
| `cyclone` | **CYCLONE** | An atmospheric cargo lifter run far beyond passenger limits. | Anything can be repurposed when certification is removed. |
| `capacitor` | **CAPACITOR** | A jury-rigged bank that stores charge while no target is present. | Peace is treated as ammunition waiting to happen. |
| `pressgang` | **PRESS GANG** | A battlefield conscription rig that raises temporary labor from corpses. | The darkest Pirate technology exposes how freedom rhetoric can conceal trafficking. |
| `privateer` | **PRIVATEER** | A gun whose targeting system carries an unofficial letter of marque. | Every kill is also a financial raid on a rival commander. |
| `bloodprice` | **BLOOD PRICE** | A weapon purchased with enclave survival rather than money. | Pirates can convert the one resource that should not be fungible. |
| `carronade` | **CARRONADE** | A capital ship's gun emplaced with its deck and heat problems intact. | Overload is both signature power and forced downtime. |
| `stokehold` | **STOKEHOLD** | A ship boiler that accepts friendly weapon heat instead of allowing guns to jam. | A reckless fleet becomes a shared thermal economy. |

## Vigil Continuance Arsenal

| Stable ID | Tower | Historical origin | Why the mechanic belongs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `railgun` | **RAILGUN** | A Continuance kinetic solution kept because it remains mathematically sufficient. | No ritual, proc, or gamble: only a solved firing equation. |
| `singularity` | **SINGULARITY** | A Federation containment core rebuilt on a Machine chassis after lawful ownership became impossible to establish. | Cross-origin technology raises the question of whether tools remember political allegiance. |
| `vault` | **VAULT** | A resource-allocation node that turns verified throughput into local currency. | Machine economy is accounting made physical. |
| `dronebay` | **DRONE BAY** | A mobile coverage system descended from autonomous inspection swarms. | The only fixed structure whose effective territory moves. |
| `foundry` | **FOUNDRY** | A repair ecology that forges bodies to engage threats directly. | Machine manufacturing becomes a temporary army. |
| `echo` | **ECHO** | A repeat node that models and reissues neighboring attacks. | The Vigil learns through adjacency rather than inspiration. |
| `quake` | **QUAKE** | A crustal maintenance hammer repurposed as field-wide ground control. | Old infrastructure makes the whole defended side its range. |
| `pylon` | **PYLON** | A local lattice keystone that increases the value of linked Machine hardware. | Machine strength is topology, not individual heroism. |
| `replicator` | **REPLICATOR** | An autonomous construction precedent that builds another approved emplacement. | Its true cost is territory and the political right to reproduce. |
| `nullfield` | **NULL FIELD** | A diagnostic volume that forces systems down to walking, armor, and mortality. | It does not overpower abilities; it refuses to recognize them. |
| `quadmount` | **QUAD MOUNT** | Four independent targeting solvers on one heavy bed. | Machine doctrine handles swarm and elite through deterministic parallelism. |
| `reactor` | **REACTOR** | A high-rated lattice core that substitutes infrastructure for local network geometry. | The Machine endgame is not a bigger gun but a better system. |
## Cross-origin exceptions are intentional

Several towers visibly contain foreign technology. The Human `ARC` and `RECLAIMER`, Machine `SINGULARITY`, Pirate mixed-origin arsenal, and Federation use of Echo Reversal should not be cleaned into single-faction purity. They demonstrate the setting's main rule: technology travels more easily than ideology, and users inherit consequences they may not understand.

## Upgrade and specialization copy

Upgrade names may remain punchy, but branch descriptions should identify the doctrinal choice:

- **scale versus precision**;
- **speed versus continuity**;
- **consent versus emergency authority**;
- **yield versus long-term stability**;
- **freedom versus accountability**;
- **replication versus individuality**.

This gives each mechanical branch an in-world argument rather than a random escalation adjective.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/worlds-battlefields-and-planets.md -->

# Worlds, Battlefields, and Planets

The reviewed game snapshot is `majieddd/majieddd.github.io@046fc38634fadf13148ef9ad3aa208417f28f2de`. It generates five systems, seven worlds per system, two contested worlds per system, one commander seat, and deterministic routes from a campaign seed.

## Names are registries, not random alien syllables

The current system roots (CORVUS, ARDENT, TALLOW REACH, THE SPINDLE, BLACKGLASS, HELIX, MOURNE, VAST) and world roots (Kerath, Vell, Ashkar, Ordos, Myrren, Tessaly, Cauldron, Hollow, Ninefold, Brack, Solace, Ixion, Tarsis, Umber, Perrin, Vaunt, Cinderfall, Quill, Sabre, Threnody, Gallow, Wexen, Orrery, Lume) are **survey registry names**. Some came from early Human astronomy, some from Pirate route ledgers, and some from translated Machine records. They are not claims about the native culture of every world.

A final world name combines:

- registry root;
- system and world index;
- local population name, when known;
- current controlling banner;
- recovered native or precursor name, if authenticated.

The short map can keep the generated registry name. The dossier should show the deeper layers.

## World Identity Packet

Every generated world can receive consistent lore from fields the game already stores:

| Existing field | Lore output |
|---|---|
| `system name` | Which Lattice pentad and route history the world belongs to. |
| `world name/id` | Stable registry identity across saves. |
| `kind` | Civil node, charter fortress, industrial forge, or Vigil nest. |
| `owner` | Current controlling claim, not total cultural identity. |
| `contestedBy` | Which other claim is legally or militarily active. |
| `seat` | Whether the world carries system command authority. |
| `map` | The physical or infrastructural topology that shapes battle. |
| `arena modifier` | A local environmental, economic, or jurisdictional condition. |
| `denizens/roster` | Which Vigil functions still operate there. |
| `boon` | The recoverable institutional or technical advantage the world produces. |
| `stars` | How complete and stable the player's victory was. |

A world blurb should be generated from those facts instead of from an unrelated paragraph.

## World kinds

| Code | Canon identity | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `standard` | Civil Node | Ordinary habitation, archive, route, or stewardship jurisdiction. |
| `fortress` | Charter Fortress | Holds defensive depth or a command registry. |
| `forge` | Industrial Forge | Converts local matter and energy into strategic production. |
| `nest` | Vigil Nest | Legacy Old Weather enforcement is dense, active, and indifferent to the banner. |

## Battlefields

| Battlefield | Classification | Canon history |
|---|---|---|
| `spine` **THE SPINE** | Barren survey trunk | A surviving linear Lattice backbone used to teach new commanders the clean geometry of jurisdiction. |
| `delta` **THE DELTA** | Flooded pelagic distributor | Two isolated channels built around an Old Weather water-management bank. |
| `narrows` **THE NARROWS** | Collapsed transit choke | Three surviving emplacement alcoves force local defense without mutual support. |
| `shattered` **SHATTERED** | Severance fracture field | The ground is broken into isolated islands by an ancient gate-shear event. |
| `crossroads` **CROSSROADS** | Dual route interchange | Two claims and two lanes cross around a monument whose original authority is no longer recognized. |
| `coil` **THE COIL** | Reactor cooling loop | A closed industrial circuit where heat, pursuit, and return paths overlap. |
| `expanse` **THE EXPANSE** | Open receiver plain | An exposed survey array with almost no natural cover and unusually long sightlines. |
| `rift` **THE RIFT** | Unstable gate shear | A broken translation channel that creates discontinuous threat geometry. |
| `confluence` **CONFLUENCE** | Three-party jurisdiction junction | Three command records are simultaneously valid and none can erase the others. |
| `crown` **THE CROWN** | Ceremonial command ring | An old authority node designed to place every claimant under observation. |
| `carousel` **CAROUSEL** | Rotating route scheduler | Traffic was once assigned dynamically; modern commanders experience it as shifting pressure. |
| `orrery` **THE ORRERY** | Navigation model made battlefield | A physical model of local orbital and gate relationships that still controls access. |
| `lattice` **THE LATTICE** | Exposed network mesh | The underlying connection graph is visible enough that placement becomes constitutional topology. |
| `causeway` **THE CAUSEWAY** | Archive bridge | A narrow connection between separated jurisdictions whose loss would erase lawful continuity. |
| `anvil` **THE ANVIL** | Foundry plate | A Machine industrial foundation built to survive impacts and continuous heavy manufacture. |
| `lance` **THE LANCE** | Long beamline corridor | An accelerator and calibration route that rewards exact range and alignment. |
| `skew` **THE SKEW** | Misregistered gravity frame | The map and the local field disagree about straight lines. |
| `strait` **THE STRAIT** | Opposed flow corridor | Traffic streams were designed to pass rather than fight; war turns that efficiency against itself. |
| `loom` **THE LOOM** | Route-weaving control plane | Multiple path threads become one legal and logistical fabric. |
| `ossuary` **THE OSSUARY** | State-deletion jurisdiction | Its archive rejects Echo Reversal, so the dead remain dead and every kill loses strategic afterlife. |
| `atoll` **THE ATOLL** | Ring habitat and central citadel | A circular settlement ecology whose center owns the life-support charter. |
| `maelstrom` **THE MAELSTROM** | Generated many-seat overload | A Lattice node that admits too many simultaneous claims and solves the conflict as a twenty-seat arena. |
## Arena modifiers as history

Arena modifiers are not arbitrary difficulty toggles. They are the surviving operating condition of the jurisdiction:

- **Swift Current:** traffic acceleration or local movement field remains active.
- **Ironclad:** the node issues protective matter to every authorized chassis, including the Vigil.
- **Gold Rush:** salvage law pays high bounties for verified removals.
- **Scarcity:** a depleted or embargoed economy cannot pay ordinary recovery rates.
- **Deep War:** the jurisdiction has already escalated before the commanders arrive.

Every modifier should gain one short archival cause and one current political consequence.

## Planet art and environmental identity

Planet portraits should be driven by a compact environmental tag set rather than faction color alone:

- gravity class;
- atmosphere and pressure;
- hydrology;
- biosphere status;
- settlement type;
- industrial signature;
- precursor/Machine signature;
- current faction claim;
- route importance.

Faction-holder variants may add infrastructure and heraldry, but they should not recolor the entire planet as if politics changed geology.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/boons-modifiers-and-scenarios.md -->

# Boons, Arena Modifiers, and Scenarios

The campaign's secondary systems should read as consequences of world history, not isolated modifiers. Their stable IDs and mechanics remain code-authoritative.

## Arena modifiers

| ID | Canon condition |
|---|---|
| `swift` **SWIFT CURRENT** | Movement infrastructure remains overdriven; every force inherits the same acceleration risk. |
| `ironclad` **IRONCLAD** | The jurisdiction still issues protective matter to every recognized chassis, including the Vigil. |
| `goldrush` **GOLD RUSH** | Local salvage law pays unusually high recovery bounties, attracting commanders and scavengers alike. |
| `scarcity` **SCARCITY** | Embargo, depletion, or broken accounting reduces what confirmed kills can recover. |
| `deepwar` **DEEP WAR** | The jurisdiction has already escalated before the commanders arrive; legacy threat assumptions begin one step higher. |
| `thinline` **THIN LINE** | Life support, evacuation capacity, or civil legitimacy is already critically depleted. |
| `warchest` **WAR CHEST** | Competing claimants arrive with pre-positioned reserves because the worlds strategic value was known in advance. |
| `frenzy` **FRENZY** | Vigil scheduling has compressed its response intervals after repeated unauthorized access. |

## Victory boons

Boons are institutions, charters, ecological adaptations, and recovered procedures. They are not magical loot detached from the conquered world. The four ordinary map powers each have five boons; the secret Vigil does not normally own campaign worlds, so it recovers other powers' infrastructure instead of supplying a fifth world-boon set.

| ID | Origin | World kind | Canon explanation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `h_refit` **FIELD REFIT** | human | standard | A Human salvage charter makes the first duplicate systems cheap enough to standardize. |
| `h_lastline` **LAST OF THE LINE** | human | fortress | A defense charter converts existential pressure into concentrated combat output. |
| `h_salvage` **SALVAGE RIGHTS** | human | forge | A legal-industrial regime allows rapid sale, reuse, and ascension of captured systems. |
| `h_hardpoints` **HARD POINTS** | human | nest | A Human foothold learns to survive Vigil disruption through redundant physical infrastructure. |
| `h_integration` **INTEGRATION** | human | apex | The decisive Human reward is broader doctrine, not a single alien superweapon. |
| `l_vigil` **THE LONG WATCH** | light | standard | A standing Accord relief network restores defensive depth after each survived wave. |
| `l_oath` **THE OATH** | light | fortress | A precisely scoped survival vow allows one otherwise lethal breach to be absorbed. |
| `l_reliquary` **RELIQUARY** | light | forge | Collective memory makes repeated ascension cheaper and more meaningful. |
| `l_aureole` **AUREOLE** | light | nest | Authenticated support fields extend farther and make status effects more coherent. |
| `l_eternal` **ETERNAL SERVICE** | light | apex | A deliberately controversial continuation charter strengthens echoes while exposing the danger of consent without end dates. |
| `x_digest` **DIGESTION** | xeno | standard | Each death increases the local efficiency of Compact extraction. |
| `x_carapace` **CARAPACE** | xeno | fortress | A repeatedly harvested system evolves resistance to enemy mustering. |
| `x_render` **RENDERING** | xeno | forge | Matter, damage, death, and currency are processed through one extraction economy. |
| `x_brood` **BROODSWELL** | xeno | nest | A local brood converts surplus mass into heavier summoned bodies. |
| `x_consume` **TOTAL CONSUMPTION** | xeno | apex | The Compact identifies the largest surviving assets as the highest-value Yield. |
| `p_plunder` **PLUNDER** | pirate | standard | A route network redirects value before any state can tax or reclaim it. |
| `p_scuttle` **SCUTTLE** | pirate | fortress | Pirate law treats fixed assets as temporary and rewards rapid liquidation. |
| `p_contraband` **CONTRABAND** | pirate | forge | Unofficial supply chains make repetition cheaper than lawful procurement allows. |
| `p_dark` **THE DARK ANSWERS** | pirate | nest | Pirate navigators exploit Vigil timing and swarm paths without truly commanding them. |
| `p_marque` **LETTERS OF MARQUE** | pirate | apex | Unrecognized authority becomes real when enough armed parties honor it. |

## Scenario modes

| ID | Campaign meaning |
|---|---|
| `assault` **ASSAULT** | Seize a jurisdiction from a rival claim while the Vigil treats both sides as unauthorized. |
| `swarm` **SWARM** | Contain a Vigil density surge whose maintenance logic is reproducing faster than either claimant can negotiate. |
| `overrun` **OVERRUN** | Recover a collapsing node after its civil defenses and archive layers have already failed. |
| `vigil` **VIGIL / ENDLESS** | Submit to an open-ended Old Weather audit in which no political victory condition is recognized. |
| `renegade_hunt` **RENEGADE HUNT** | Remove an outlaw commander or corrupted claim operating under your own banners credentials. |
| `swarm_defense` **SWARM DEFENSE** | Defend a world you already hold when its legacy enforcement routines reactivate. |
| `coop_reinforcement` **CO-OP REINFORCEMENT** | Answer an allied request where preserving the partner matters more than replacing their claim. |

## Writing rule

A battle card should state **what changed here**, **who benefits**, and **why the condition persists**. Do not use an arena modifier as a generic adjective when an existing world field can supply a specific cause.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/dialogue-and-character-voice.md -->

# Dialogue and Character Voice Bible

The current dialogue system has commander openers, faction reply pools, and a few special pairs. The retrofit should preserve that lightweight architecture while replacing generic threats with lines driven by history and world state.

## Dialogue assembly order

A pre-battle exchange should draw from these layers in order:

1. **relationship line** for commanders with shared history;
2. **world claim line** based on owner, contested status, seat, or rescue state;
3. **battlefield line** based on map topology and Vigil function;
4. **faction doctrine line** only when no specific history exists;
5. **generic tactical line** as the final fallback.

This prevents every Federation commander from speaking like a priest, every Xeno like an animal, every Pirate like a joke, every Human like a generic soldier, and every Machine like the same cold computer.

## Canon-sensitive language

- Federation characters may be coercive, but official copy must distinguish **consent**, **emergency protection**, and **hardliner overreach**. Remove lines claiming everyone must surrender free will as a constitutional ideal.
- Xeno dialogue should emphasize dependency, contracts, Yield, memory, hierarchy, and client systems. Hunger language belongs mainly to Mawlord, Ulgrim, and the Voracious Brood.
- Pirate dialogue should distinguish abolitionists, sanctuary captains, salvagers, privateers, and traffickers.
- Human dialogue should reveal different polities and professions rather than treating Humanity as one military culture.
- Machine dialogue should expose distinct mission interpretations. The playable banner and the neutral enforcement waves are one civilization named the Vigil (the name Parallel is retired), but no single process speaks for the whole Old Weather.

## Commander voices and sample lines

| ID | Voice constraints | Sample opener | Sample result line |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cadre` | Plain, procedural, restrained. Describes risk instead of destiny. | "Threat profile entered. Build the line you can explain." | "The node held. Record what worked." |
| `vanta` | Cites files, precedents, probabilities, and missing records. | "Your claim cites an archive that ends three pages too early." | "Filed: victory, provenance intact, interpretation pending." |
| `korrin` | Prices, tonnage, lead time, spare parts, contractual leverage. | "Your empire has one route in and I already bought the spare parts." | "A victory that cannot be supplied is only a delay." |
| `nyx` | Fast, intimate, reckless; speaks to machines as if daring them. | "The governor says this system cannot run any hotter. Good." | "Rating exceeded. Consequence accepted." |
| `orin` | Diagnostic, tactile, impatient with mysticism. | "I do not need to understand your relic before I learn where it breaks." | "Leave the wreck. I can make it local." |
| `vess` | Legal certainty, measured threats, geographic language. | "This charter names the people who chose to stay. You do not speak over them." | "Ground retained. Authority remains with those living on it." |
| `seraph` | Warm, formal, plural; never says surrender when consent is meant. | "We offer protection, witness, and a choice that survives the battle." | "The Accord holds only while the signatories do." |
| `aurelia` | Musical cadence, names the wounded, avoids abstraction. | "I know every voice your last barrage interrupted." | "Count the living before you count the victory." |
| `lumen` | Precise, calm, protective; frames every claim as a scope and permission. | "Your command signature is valid for another jurisdiction, not this one." | "Scope verified. Breach denied." |
| `cantor` | Patient rhetorical questions, reframing, deliberate silence. | "Before you fire, tell me which future this shot makes possible." | "Silence is not agreement. I will wait for the answer." |
| `halder` | Grave, economical, speaks in obligations and thresholds. | "I will not let you exterminate them while they decide whether to trust us." | "Protected is not possessed. Remember the distinction." |
| `sevra` | Contractual, clinical, calls corpses estates and echoes continuations. | "Death closed the body, not the obligation." | "The estate has been transferred to active service." |
| `mawlord` | Sensory, material, hungry but not mindless. | "You call it waste because you cannot hear matter asking to be used." | "Nothing lost. Everything incorporated." |
| `thrax` | Uses we inconsistently; shifts between intimate singular and vast plural. | "We remember choosing this. Some of us remember refusing." | "The vote is complete. The disagreement remains inside us." |
| `vorn` | Agricultural, epidemiological, patient. | "Your victory already depends on what I planted beneath it." | "The battle ends. The dependency flowers." |
| `ulgrim` | Selective, evaluative, dismissive of anything common. | "Send me the mind your civilization cannot replace." | "Common bodies pass. Singular ones are retained." |
| `rake` | Dry, transactional, charming when lying. | "No route belongs to you just because your fleet blocks it." | "Toll paid, cordon broken, destination still free." |
| `scarlet` | Immediate, kinetic, contemptuous of plans longer than the fight. | "A wall is only a prison that has not chosen its prisoners yet." | "Burn the claim before someone rebuilds it." |
| `grist` | Appraises everything; jokes in material grades and resale value. | "That sacred hull has three compatible couplings and terrible resale etiquette." | "Nothing abandoned stays powerless for long." |
| `cinder` | Fatalistic, concise, treats destruction as cleanup. | "I can save the gate or save what is coming through it. Pick one." | "Route denied. Survivors can argue later." |
| `dregg` | Commanding, mocking, uses debt and ammunition interchangeably. | "I do not need your flag. I need your ammunition schedule." | "You kept the world. I kept the war." |
| `axiom` | Observational, revision-oriented, occasionally startlingly personal. | "Your doctrine contains a conclusion its evidence does not. I would like to test it." | "Revision accepted. Identity retained." |
| `nyx_r` | Cool, incremental, speaks in versions and convergence. | "The original called this courage. The data calls it thermal debt." | "Convergence achieved without worshiping the failure." |
| `lumen_r` | Audit language, permissions, attack surfaces. | "Trust request received. Evidence insufficient. Continue attacking." | "Intrusion incorporated. Permission still denied." |
| `mawlord_r` | Sparse at first, increasingly associative and unsettling. | "I was given no hunger. Your battle is teaching me one." | "Dataset expanded. Moral classification unresolved." |
| `dregg_r` | Forensic accounting, causal chains, no bravado. | "Your freedom has recurring expenses and one undisclosed owner." | "Ledger balanced. Mythology excluded." |
## Required scene pools

Each commander should ultimately receive short lines for:

- first meeting;
- same-faction dispute;
- each of the four rival factions;
- system commander seat;
- contested three-way world;
- Vigil nest;
- fortress and forge;
- one battlefield they understand unusually well;
- ability activation;
- unit rescue;
- one-star, two-star, and three-star victory;
- defeat and return;
- Vigil reveal;
- Maelstrom endgame.

The machine-readable commander registry contains motive and contradiction fields that can be used to generate drafts, but final dialogue should be hand-reviewed for voice and continuity.

---

<!-- Source module: docs/game/art-direction-and-asset-brief.md -->

# Art Direction and Asset Revamp Brief


This brief translates the canon into production rules for commander portraits, faction crests, units, towers, enemies, planet plates, and dossier art. It supplements the stable-ID [Asset Revamp Matrix](asset-revamp-matrix.md); mechanics remain code-authoritative.

## Global art rule

Every asset must communicate three things before it communicates decoration:

1. **builder or institutional origin**;
2. **the mechanic or battlefield function**;
3. **the political cost or contradiction**.

A faction color may aid readability, but it must not erase species, material, environmental, or institutional diversity.

## Five visual systems

| Power | Shape and material language | Recurring evidence of history | Avoid |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Humans** | Adaptors, pressure seals, modular hardpoints, civic labels, industrial cyan and weathered alloys. | Foreign components mounted on understandable frames; Earth, lunar, Martian, Belt, and Jovian standards visibly coexist. | One homogeneous Earth military or sleek generic sci-fi navy. |
| **Federation** | Open circles, scoped halos, witness threads, radiant amber/gold, transparent boundaries, many-species interfaces. | Consent seals, memory chains, emergency-key markings, and visible room for individual identity. | Literal angels as the universal species, church-copy ornament, or imagery that presents coercion as the official ideal. |
| **Xenos / Compact** | No single morphology. House overlays, ownership marks, dependency ports, purple as UI affiliation rather than skin color. | Patents, tithe seals, client-caste interfaces, grafts, noetic extraction architecture, and old severance damage. | A single evil alien race or one shared organic aesthetic. |
| **Pirates** | Patchwork pressure environments, route tattoos, cargo geometry, red/copper hazard paint, incompatible components kept visible. | Sanctuary markings, disputed salvage tags, cut credentials, quarantine scars, and mixed-species accommodations. | Theme-park piracy, universal skull motifs, or comic relief by default. |
| **Machines** | Function-first geometry, geological and ecological integration, silver/cyan Vigil revisions, old teal/stone Old Weather structures. | Version seams, mission-law inscriptions, repair strata, archive checksums, and divergent node jurisprudence. | Generic exterminator robots or assuming every Machine shares one body language. |

## Machine distinction

- **Old Weather:** ancient, distributed, oceanic/crustal/ecological, rarely humanoid.
- **Vigil:** custodial and enforcement chassis whose original civil function remains readable beneath weaponization.
- **Vigil:** modern political Machines who preserve revision history and deliberately display personhood choices.

## Commander portrait briefs

| ID | Must show | Must avoid |
|---|---|---|
| `cadre` | Neutral modular field uniform assembled from all five origins; no faction crest; worn training slate. | Do not make Cadre visually bland: the tension is deliberate interoperability, not absence of identity. |
| `vanta` | Archive coat with transparent provenance panes, timestamp marks, and a portable evidence lattice. | Avoid conspiracy-wall clutter; she is disciplined archival intelligence, not a paranoid detective. |
| `korrin` | Pressure-rated logistics harness, cargo seals from Earth/Moon/Mars/Belt, and one standardized coupling held like a badge. | Avoid generic merchant styling; he is infrastructure power. |
| `nyx` | Heat-scored ascension rig, exposed governor bypasses, asymmetric cooling lines, confident burn scars. | Avoid magical lightning; every visual risk should look engineered. |
| `orin` | Mixed-origin repair frame with visibly incompatible tools made interoperable by adapters. | Avoid pristine lab-coat imagery; he works inside damaged systems. |
| `vess` | Marshal coat over habitat emergency armor, civil charter ribbons, geographic map projection. | Avoid nationalistic Earth-only insignia; she represents frontier jurisdiction. |
| `seraph` | Radiant field harness made of many individually distinct consent seals orbiting without merging. | Avoid angel wings as literal biology; halo geometry should read as treaty infrastructure. |
| `aurelia` | Casualty-memory choir filaments, names or voiceprints of the recovered, protective rather than regal posture. | Avoid faceless saint imagery; grief and individuality must remain visible. |
| `lumen` | Layered ward architecture, authentication glyphs, narrow luminous apertures, visible permission boundaries. | Avoid omniscient all-seeing-eye cliche. |
| `cantor` | Diplomatic voice lattice, empty space in composition, one hand inviting and one controlling an emotional waveform. | Avoid preacher staging; he is a negotiator with unsettling influence. |
| `halder` | Heavy evacuation armor, threshold markers, emergency command key carried as a burden. | Avoid uncomplicated paladin heroism; the image should ask whether rescue can be coercive. |
| `sevra` | Clinical continuity notary with estate seals, echo capsules, and death-contract ledgers. | Avoid undead sorceress shorthand; her horror is administrative. |
| `mawlord` | Massive client organism built from salvage metabolism, with visible old ownership marks cut away. | Avoid generic monster rage; intelligence and emancipation motive must show. |
| `thrax` | Multiple bodies or faces linked by an imperfect shared field, some looking away from the collective center. | Avoid a single insect queen as the whole character. |
| `vorn` | Ecological engineer in living cultivation armor, seed/debt instruments, slow invasive growth. | Avoid simple poison villain styling; dependency is designed as stewardship. |
| `ulgrim` | Elite-capture adjudicator bearing memory reliquaries from singular opponents. | Avoid indiscriminate gore; Ulgrim is selective and curatorial. |
| `rake` | Route charts stitched into coat and hull plates, quarantine tags, dry confidence. | Avoid cartoon pirate costume; the route network is his real regalia. |
| `scarlet` | Breaching gear, severed chains, recent damage, forward motion that ignores stable ground. | Avoid sexualized reaver tropes; emphasize anti-institutional velocity. |
| `grist` | Salvage-jury robes made from inspection tags, material samples, and legal stamps. | Avoid junk-pile comedy; Grist is a serious property-law power. |
| `cinder` | Scuttle specialist with remote gate charges and scorched route maps. | Avoid pyromaniac caricature; destruction is his grim containment policy. |
| `dregg` | Privateer admiral with no official flag, hidden ledgers, and a fleet reflected behind him like a state he denies. | Avoid simple pirate king grandeur; hypocrisy must be visible. |
| `axiom` | Clean but visibly revised chassis; version seams retained as honors; first self-authored sentence engraved internally. | Avoid generic chrome robot monarch. |
| `nyx_r` | Cold, orderly mirror of Nyx with redundant cooling and deliberate asymmetry inherited from copied data. | Avoid making it merely a blue recolor of Nyx. |
| `lumen_r` | Security chassis bearing repaired intrusion scars as learned credentials. | Avoid pristine invulnerability; earned hardening is the visual story. |
| `mawlord_r` | Sparse initial frame accumulating battlefield-derived structures unevenly, with uncertainty visible in unfinished morphology. | Avoid a robotic copy of Mawlord; it learned appetite from data. |
| `dregg_r` | Forensic economic chassis projecting causal ledgers through a stripped-down admiral silhouette. | Avoid accountant comedy; it can dismantle empires through audit. |

## Unit and tower production

- A unit portrait must show whether its relationship to the player is recruitment, asylum, contract, emancipation, copying, or mission transfer.
- A tower plate must show the core mechanic in silhouette. Range, tether, aura, beam, foundry, execution, or lattice behavior should be visually inferable before reading text.
- Cross-origin technology must remain visible. The Human `reclaimer`, Machine `singularity`, Federation use of Echo Reversal, and Pirate mixed systems are evidence of history, not art inconsistencies.
- Vigil dossier art should pair the combat silhouette with a faint reconstruction of its original survey, repair, containment, access-control, or emergency function.

## Planet and world plates

Planet art is built from environmental tags first, then political overlays:

1. gravity and scale;
2. atmosphere and pressure;
3. water/ice distribution;
4. biosphere condition;
5. settlement and industry;
6. precursor or Machine signature;
7. route importance;
8. current and contested claims.

A faction-holder variant adds infrastructure, traffic, fortification, or heraldry. It does not recolor geology. A contested world should show two incompatible systems occupying the same environment rather than splitting the planet into simple color halves.

## Dialogue portrait states

Every commander eventually needs five portrait states: neutral dossier, pre-battle assertion, under-pressure fracture, recognition/alliance, and faction-ending choice. Mirror Trial portraits must share identifiable source data with the namesake without becoming a face swap.

# Archive War Mission Seeds


These missions convert real records and source-history problems into gameplay without declaring their extraordinary interpretations true. Each mission names the source layer and an exact live commander, battlefield, world kind, or tower family so writers can move directly from research to scenario design.

| ID | Mission | Layer | Commander | Map / world | Asset anchors | Premise | Objective |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AW-01` | **The 701st File** | B | `vanta` | `causeway` / `standard` | `beacon`, `mortar` | A Blue Book residual acquires meaning only when a Lattice coordinate frame is recovered. The file is authentic; the future coordinate interpretation is F. | Secure the archive bridge without destroying either the original scan or the later annotation layer. |
| `AW-02` | **Arnold Lexicon** | B/C | `cadre` | `spine` / `standard` | `flak` | Three factions build different craft profiles from one witness description because later illustrations became more influential than the original words. | Classify targets by behavior rather than inherited silhouette labels. |
| `AW-03` | **The Mantell Rule** | B | `vess` | `expanse` / `fortress` | `flak`, `pharos` | A pursuit doctrine memorializes the danger of chasing an unknown beyond sensor and vehicle limits. | Win while refusing a lure that offers combat advantage outside verified coverage. |
| `AW-04` | **Jafari Chain** | B | `vanta` | `crossroads` / `fortress` | `nullfield`, `jammer` | Records modeled on the 1976 Tehran case disagree across witness, aircraft, weapon, and command channels. | Keep each evidence stream intact while an Archonic spoof tries to merge them into one false certainty. |
| `AW-05` | **Nimitz Partition** | B | `orin` | `orrery` / `standard` | `prism`, `arc` | Eyewitness, radar, infrared, and later recollection are stored as separate objects rather than one perfect account. | Reconstruct the route without granting any single sensor total authority. |
| `AW-06` | **GoFast Geometry** | A/B | `lumen_r` | `skew` / `standard` | `chrono` | A strange-looking track becomes conventional after geometry is modeled, while exact identity remains unknown. | Score higher by resolving behavior without inventing an identity. |
| `AW-07` | **Aguadilla Split** | A/B | `lumen` | `delta` / `standard` | `monstrance` | Apparent splitting and ocean entry compete with a conventional two-object and sensor-geometry analysis. | Prevent both a Compact false-USO narrative and a Human claim that all ocean anomalies are solved. |
| `AW-08` | **Record Group 615** | A | `vanta` | `loom` / `forge` | `quartermaster` | FAA, NRC, ODNI, OSD, NSA, State, and FBI transfers arrive with incompatible metadata and release conditions. | Create a searchable common schema without deleting agency provenance. |
| `AW-09` | **The Greenewald Standard** | A/F | `grist` | `causeway` / `forge` | `vault` | A Pirate archive offers millions of pages but demands that denial letters, appeals, and missing attachments remain tradeable assets. | Publish enough to preserve trust without surrendering vulnerable sources. |
| `AW-10` | **Space Tiger Team** | A/B | `lumen_r` | `orrery` / `fortress` | `pharos`, `dronebay` | A 2023 space/transmedium detection framework becomes the ancestor of orbital anomaly deconfliction. | Identify duplicate tracks across domains without treating the word transmedium as a conclusion. |
| `AW-11` | **Seventy-Eight Frames** | A/B | `vanta` | `ossuary` / `fortress` | `sepulchre` | A Navy denial confirms responsive classified UAP photographs existed but not what they depict. | Preserve the existence of the records after the image content is deleted by a hostile archive process. |
| `AW-12` | **The Virginia Hundred** | B | `halder` | `confluence` / `standard` | `beacon`, `dronebay` | A Navy report describes roughly one hundred airborne observations and two likely uncrewed surface systems; the future operator remains unknown. | Protect civilian traffic while three factions weaponize the object count as propaganda. |
| `AW-13` | **Satellite Flare Flood** | A | `cantor` | `carousel` / `standard` | `prism`, `pharos` | A new modeling capability resolves large numbers of reports as satellite reflections. | Calm a population without allowing the Compact to hide one unrelated operation inside the mass resolution. |
| `AW-14` | **Diamond in SWIR** | B | `orin` | `lance` / `standard` | `monstrance`, `railgun` | A 2024 report describes a diamond-shaped object detectable in short-wave infrared; the public clip is descriptive, not a final analytic judgment. | Build a sensor chain that can test detection-band dependence. |
| `AW-15` | **Surface Turns** | B | `rake` | `atoll` / `standard` | `tether`, `dronebay` | A 2023 maritime clip is reported as showing turns near the ocean surface, but sensor motion and range remain contested. | Retrieve native data before a Pirate copy becomes the only surviving record. |
| `AW-16` | **Malmstrom Alert, 1975** | B | `vess` | `crown` / `fortress` | `flak`, `nullfield` | Historical records describe unknown craft reports around sensitive installations and attempted interception. | Hold the weapons site without mistaking uncertainty for permission to launch. |
| `AW-17` | **Echo Flight, 1967** | B/E | `vanta` | `ossuary` / `fortress` | `ward` | Missile-disablement claims, later testimony, missing documentation, and conventional explanations coexist. | Maintain two live interpretations while a faction tries to canonize one for political gain. |
| `AW-18` | **JAL 1628** | B | `korrin` | `expanse` / `standard` | `pharos` | A long-duration aviation report becomes a test of crew testimony, radar context, and public release. | Escort the flight and preserve independent logs through a communications blackout. |
| `AW-19` | **O Hare Aperture** | B/C | `cantor` | `atoll` / `standard` | `beacon` | An airport sighting and cloud-hole narrative become a mass-belief event with limited official data. | Prevent panic and memory convergence from turning uncertain witness accounts into one manufactured revelation. |
| `AW-20` | **Socorro Traces** | B | `orin` | `narrows` / `standard` | `canister` | A close-range report includes alleged ground traces and a vehicle-like object. | Distinguish physical trace, witness interpretation, and later symbol reconstruction. |
| `AW-21` | **Levelland Circuit** | B | `nyx` | `coil` / `forge` | `arc`, `capacitor` | Vehicle-electrical effects reported across a region compete with weather and ignition explanations. | Keep the grid running while testing whether the effect follows environment, machines, or narrative contagion. |
| `AW-22` | **Majestic Custody** | E | `sevra` | `crown` / `fortress` | `ward`, `monstrance` | A document can be archived and investigated without being authentic. | Expose the forgery while finding the real compartmentation practice it copied. |
| `AW-23` | **Project Beta** | D/E | `vanta` | `rift` / `standard` | `siren`, `ward` | A sincere witness, classified testing, alleged disinformation, and underground-base mythology contaminate one another. | Rescue the person and the evidence without validating the false total story. |
| `AW-24` | **Active Archive** | A/B | `axiom` | `lattice` / `standard` | `vault`, `echo` | Cases with too little data are retained rather than solved or discarded. | Design an archive policy that allows future learning without turning uncertainty into permanent suspicion. |

| `AW-25` | **Five Releases** | A/B | `vanta` | `lattice` / `forge` | `vault`, `echo` | Five government release batches become searchable through processed OCR, transcripts, media, and metadata, but the derivative corpus cannot replace the originals. | Reconcile 375 records without erasing release batch, source hash, processing history, or OCR uncertainty. |
| `AW-26` | **Vigil Track** | B | `orin` | `lance` / `standard` | `flak`, `railgun` | A business jet reports an object pacing it over Nevada while witness language changes and the most useful radar files remain unavailable. | Protect the flight and preserve black-program, foreign-platform, conventional, and exotic hypotheses until the evidence can separate them. |
| `AW-27` | **Media Integrity Ladder** | A/B | `lumen_r` | `shattered` / `standard` | `monstrance`, `prism` | Native sensor products, screen recordings, altered copies, witness videos, and artistic recreations are mixed into one apparent evidence feed. | Restore parent-child provenance and prevent a reconstruction from being scored as event footage. |
| `AW-28` | **Null Search Appeal** | A/E | `sevra` | `crown` / `fortress` | `nullfield`, `ward` | An agency refuses to search for a phrase because the alleged program is said not to exist, even though records may discuss the allegation itself. | Compel a lawful search without converting the appeal victory into proof that the alleged program was real. |
| `AW-29` | **Evaluation 585** | A/B | `cadre` | `causeway` / `fortress` | `pylon`, `nullfield` | An inspector-general proposal reveals what the institution intended to evaluate while the claimed benefits and capability details remain redacted. | Map the detection and counterintelligence workflow without treating classification as an extraordinary finding. |
| `AW-30` | **Diplomatic Sky** | A/B | `seraph` | `crossroads` / `standard` | `beacon`, `pharos` | State Department records preserve UAP-related reports as diplomatic, cultural, and political events across several countries, not only as sensor cases. | Translate and compare reports without stripping away local context or turning diplomatic transmission into endorsement. |
| `AW-31` | **Contactee Network** | B-D | `cantor` | `rift` / `standard` | `beacon`, `echo` | FBI files on contactees, organizations, investigators, broadcasters, skeptics, and disputed documents record how belief networks spread and were monitored. | Separate correspondence, surveillance, fraud inquiry, sincere belief, and later mythology before reconstructing the network. |
| `AW-32` | **Proposal Not Program** | A/E | `axiom` | `ossuary` / `forge` | `vault`, `foundry` | A dramatic special-access proposal is repeatedly cited as an operational recovery program even though it was never approved, funded, established, or supplied with material. | Recover the proposal history while stopping every faction from skipping the proposal-program-operation ladder. |
| `AW-33` | **Ordinary Alloy** | A/B | `grist` | `anvil` / `forge` | `reclaimer`, `canister` | A specimen with an extraordinary recovery story is tested and found consistent with ordinary industrial alloy. | Preserve chain of custody, analytical method, and negative result while searching separately for the origin of the story. |
| `AW-34` | **Refuse to Answer** | A | `vanta` | `loom` / `standard` | `echo`, `beacon` | A mandatory reporting system converted every blank field into a confident negative, manufacturing a false pattern across thousands of reports. | Reconstruct the original uncertainty states and redesign the form without destroying useful structured data. |
| `AW-35` | **Watcher Array** | A/F | `axiom` | `lattice` / `fortress` | `dronebay`, `pharos` | An event-based sensor mesh detects every moving cluster in a battlespace but initially labels each one unidentified, including known drones, reflections, fauna, and one persistent residual. | Maintain custody and characterize signatures before any faction can turn a provisional track into a declared enemy. |
| `AW-36` | **The Soda Can** | A | `lumen_r` | `causeway` / `standard` | `monstrance`, `nullfield` | A classified image becomes conspiracy evidence even though the protected information concerns the collecting platform, metadata, and sensor performance rather than the ordinary object in frame. | Declassify enough to resolve the subject while preventing the release from exposing how to evade the sensor. |
| `AW-37` | **Native Master** | A/B | `orin` | `shattered` / `forge` | `reclaimer`, `vault` | Six viral edits and three official derivatives descend from one native recording whose first hash and metadata log are missing. | Recover the memory card, establish the earliest defensible master, and preserve every fork without promoting any edit into original evidence. |

## Use rule

The historical event supplies an evidence problem, not a secret answer. The future plot may add a displaced operational truth, but the source card must display where the historical record ends and the F adaptation begins.

<!-- Source module: docs/game/asset-revamp-matrix.md -->

# Asset Revamp Matrix

The unified matrix gives writers, artists, designers, and coding agents one row per current content object. It combines stable ID, political alignment, historical origin, present mechanic, moral tension, governing document, and retrofit status.

- CSV: [`../../data/content-revamp-matrix.csv`](../../data/content-revamp-matrix.csv)
- JSON: [`../../data/content-revamp-matrix.json`](../../data/content-revamp-matrix.json)

## How teams should use it

- **Character and portrait work:** filter `category = commander`.
- **Troop and enemy dossier work:** filter `faction_unit` or `vigil_chassis`.
- **Tower visual redesign:** filter `tower`, then preserve the stable ID and mechanic.
- **Planet and battlefield art:** filter `battlefield` and combine it with the generated World Identity Packet.
- **Dialogue:** use commander motive, contradiction, relationship tags, current world claim, and campaign act.
- **Implementation review:** no row may be marked complete until its final player-facing copy links back to its canonical document.

This matrix does not replace the category-specific JSON. It is a contributor-oriented index across them.


## Production companions

- [Art Direction and Asset Revamp Brief](art-direction-and-asset-brief.md)
- [Archive War Mission Seeds](archive-war-mission-seeds.md)
- [`data/visual-asset-briefs.json`](../../data/visual-asset-briefs.json)
- [`data/archive-war-missions.json`](../../data/archive-war-missions.json)
