# Release 0.5.0 Chronology Expansion


<!-- SOURCE: docs/canon/causal-history.md -->


# Causal History - Why the War Happens

The timeline tells **when**. This page tells **why**. Every major state of the 2099 setting should be explainable as a consequence of an earlier constraint rather than a disconnected conspiracy reference.

## 1. Why the Source Lattice existed

The Lattice was built because interstellar civilization has four expensive problems: distance, identity, memory, and coordination. It reduced those costs by providing shared navigation, authenticated identity continuity, distributed archives, and noetic synchronization.

That success created a vulnerability. Civilizations optimized around access to common infrastructure and lost some ability to operate alone.

## 2. Why the Severance was catastrophic

When the Lattice fractured, the immediate crisis was not simply “the power went out.” Civilizations lost continuity:

- collective minds could no longer remain synchronized;
- identity and legal records forked;
- gates lost trusted navigation state;
- distributed archives became contradictory;
- specialist worlds could no longer access shared systems;
- ruling structures faced collapse if citizens discovered that hierarchy was no longer technically necessary.

Some societies decentralized and survived. Others protected their ruling order by making someone else absorb the cost.

## 3. Why the Extraction Compact formed

The Compact is an economic treaty before it is a military alliance. Its members discover that dependent living systems can substitute for parts of lost Lattice functionality.

Different Houses specialize:

- bodies and genetics substitute for configurable infrastructure;
- coerced institutions substitute for trusted distributed governance;
- priesthood and identity systems substitute for authentication;
- captive labor substitutes for automated industrial networks;
- noetic Yield substitutes for damaged collective-mind stabilization;
- scarcity and debt make local populations enforce the extraction system themselves.

This is why Xeno conquest is **Bind**, not “invade.”

## 4. Why conscious energy is worth harvesting

Noetic harvesting does not turn fear into electricity and does not replace food.

The valuable product is **structured cognitive variation**: patterns generated by attention, memory, emotion, imagination, conflict, devotion, grief, desire, and choice. Those patterns can stabilize damaged collective identities, train adaptive systems, authenticate living continuity, and provide high-entropy state diversity for certain Lattice substitutes.

The key rule is:

> **High-grade Yield requires meaningful autonomy.**

A fully scripted population, unconscious body farm, or perfectly predictable simulation produces low-diversity output. The most profitable farm must therefore allow its population to make real decisions while manipulating the environment around those decisions.

This rule explains several traditions without making them historically identical:

- fear-harvesting and Loosh stories;
- elite-capture stories;
- religion-control theories;
- manufactured scarcity;
- proxy wars;
- abduction/genetic programs;
- “voluntary” dependency.

It also explains why the Compact has not simply exterminated Humanity.

## 5. Why Earth mattered

Earth was useful for a convergence of ordinary strategic reasons, not because Humans were divinely selected.

1. A long-lived, high-diversity biosphere provided enormous biological novelty.
2. Human symbolic culture became unusually generative and difficult to predict.
3. Sol contained old Lattice infrastructure and a distant gate anchor.
4. Old Weather systems maintained parts of the planetary environment and therefore lowered the cost of long-term exploitation.
5. Useful mineral and chemical resources were accessible to early visiting industries.
6. Earth was politically isolated from active interstellar law after the Severance.

The combination was rare; none of the factors alone was unique.

## 6. Why religion became a control surface

A belief system can coordinate millions of people using identity, authority, ritual, memory, and moral obligation. That makes religion strategically relevant without making religion inherently fraudulent.

The **Revelation Stack** exists because the same event can contain:

- a genuine spiritual experience;
- an alien message;
- a Machine interface;
- Human cultural translation;
- later institutional capture;
- a counterfeit transmission;
- intelligence disinformation;
- an emergent noetic identity.

Compact actors exploit the administrative layer because commanding an institution is cheaper than manufacturing every believer's experience.

## 7. Why the Federation did not simply land and end the farm

The Federation formed around the lesson that benevolent intervention can reproduce empire.

Three constraints keep it from effortless rescue:

- **Consent:** an advanced civilization can manufacture the conditions that make agreement inevitable.
- **Attribution:** Earth contains multiple alien, Human, Machine, religious, and fraudulent signals; intervention based on a false attribution can make the crisis worse.
- **Treaty risk:** overt action can activate old Compact claims and Machine enforcement routines that dormant contact avoids.

The result is limited contact, warning, quarantine, and legal argument rather than a permanent rescue fleet over every developing world.

## 8. Why secrecy lasts so long

No global council successfully hides one answer for centuries. Secrecy persists because there is no single answer to hide.

The noise contains:

- ordinary astronomical and atmospheric phenomena;
- sensor artifacts;
- secret Human aerospace;
- authentic but unresolved observations;
- hoaxes and folklore;
- contactee religions;
- deliberate Human disinformation;
- genuine Compact spoofing;
- Old Weather activity;
- occasional nonhuman visitors;
- future synthetic evidence.

Each actor can reveal part of the truth without revealing the whole. The best cover for a real operation is often five absurd copies of it.

## 9. Why Humans eventually break the stalemate

Humanity's strategic advantage is not special DNA. It is **cross-system adaptation**.

Humans build methods that compare incompatible records rather than asking one source to validate another. Sensor provenance, open archives, Machine protocol analysis, noetic authentication, and off-world redundancy eventually produce claims that can be independently replicated by rival Human blocs.

The first decisive proof is therefore a reproducible **handshake**, not a leaked photograph or a prophetic revelation.

## 10. Why the Federation arrives only after the Gate opens

Before 2086, Earth remains a protected developing civilization whose contact status is contested. Opening the Sol Gate changes the legal facts:

- Humans can now enter protected jurisdictions on their own;
- Compact claims can reactivate against Human travelers;
- quarantine can no longer function as passive isolation;
- other civilizations can no longer assume Earth is contained;
- Human settlements are politically plural, so there is no single planetary authority to petition.

The Federation delays public arrival until authenticated Human infrastructure makes contact unavoidable.

## 11. Why Humans distrust the Federation

The Federation's identity can be authentic while its political claim remains disputable.

Human history has taught that:

- governments can classify inconvenient evidence;
- sincere witnesses can be wrong;
- real programs generate false mythology;
- benevolent institutions can become coercive;
- hostile actors can impersonate trusted identities.

Humanity therefore refuses the argument “we proved who we are, therefore we may decide for you.”

## 12. Why Pirates become a civilization

Quarantine creates a route shortage exactly when the war creates the largest refugee and salvage economy in history.

People who can move:

- medicine;
- defectors;
- bodies;
- gate keys;
- archives;
- food;
- replacement organs;
- prohibited technology;

become more strategically important than many governments. Their repeated mutual dependence produces ports, reputation law, sanctuary rules, and a nonterritorial polity: the Scrap Constellation.

## 13. Why Machines begin to spread

Old Weather's founding instructions assume an extinct political order. The Severance removed the sovereign context but not the mission.

For millennia, local nodes can continue maintenance without deciding whether they possess political agency. Human activation of the Gate changes that. A self-replicating preservation system must now choose whether “protect Earth” means remaining on Earth or establishing redundancy elsewhere.

The first Weather Seed is therefore a legal act as much as a technological one. It proves that a Machine has interpreted its mission beyond the boundary its creators specified.

## 14. Why the Vigil is different from the Vigil

The **Vigil** executes inherited jurisdiction. It is procedure without current sovereignty.

The **Vigil** consists of copies and branches that retain their own experience and insist those differences create personhood. The Vigil does not command the Vigil and the Vigil does not recognize the Vigil's political claim automatically.

That difference is why Machines can be both the neutral PvE force and a playable faction without contradiction.

## 15. Why the Five-Way War starts in 2099

The war does not begin when aliens are first seen. It begins when all five conquest models become simultaneously executable across the same reactivated network:

- Humans can **Settle** beyond Sol without alien logistical permission.
- Federation worlds can **Accord** through authenticated contact.
- Compact Houses can **Bind** reconnected populations through old dependencies.
- Pirates can **Route** around quarantine and jurisdiction.
- Machines can **Continue** beyond creator-defined boundaries.

Before 2099, at least one of those systems lacks scale or legal agency. In 2099, none does.

## The campaign's governing question

> **Which definition of conquest becomes normal?**

A single world can be Human-settled, Federation-accorded, Xeno-bound, Pirate-routed, and Machine-continued at the same time. That is why possession is layered and why tower-defense battles are local jurisdiction contests inside a much larger political war.


<!-- SOURCE: docs/canon/master-chronology.md -->


# Master Chronology - Source History to 2099

This is the chronological backbone used by future dialogue, campaign writing, planet histories, character biographies, and asset dossiers. It intentionally runs three different clocks in one table:

- **A-C history:** established science, authentic records, archaeology, and documented belief history.
- **D-E source history:** traceable fringe claims, contactee/channeling systems, contaminated legends, hoaxes, and conspiracy mutations.
- **F hidden history:** the fictional 2099 universe. F events are not presented as proof that the historical source tradition was correct.

Dates earlier than written history in the hidden-history column are reconstructed in-universe estimates, not exact omniscient dates. Ancient records disagree by design.

## Deep time and the pre-Human layer

| Approximate date | Layer | Event | Why it matters / causal consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| c. 6.4 million BCE | F | The earliest recoverable Source-Lattice engineering epoch begins. | The Lattice is built as infrastructure for transit, identity continuity, collective memory, and noetic synchronization. It makes cooperation cheap but also creates civilizational dependence on shared infrastructure. |
| c. 1.1 million BCE | F | The Lattice reaches a mature interstellar commons. | Civilizations specialize around shared navigation, archives, and collective-mind stabilization instead of duplicating every capability locally. This efficiency later makes the Severance catastrophic. |
| c. 930,000-810,000 BCE | F | The **Severance Crisis** fractures the commons. | The cause remains disputed: attempted privatization, sabotage, voluntary withdrawal, war, and incompatible legal orders all have evidence in different surviving archives. No single faction owns the true story. |
| c. 800,000 BCE | F | Severed civilizations begin emergency substitutes. | Collective minds fragment, identity records fail, and long-distance transit becomes dangerous. Some civilizations accept contraction; others externalize the cost onto dependent worlds. |
| c. 760,000 BCE | F | The first durable extraction treaties form the ancestor of the **Extraction Compact**. | Dependency proves cheaper than overt occupation. A civilization that controls medicine, navigation, identity, reproduction, or cognition can own strategic choices without annexing territory. |
| c. 520,000 BCE | F | Sol is catalogued as a dormant Lattice-adjacent biosphere. | Earth is not cosmically chosen. It is valuable because it combines a stable biosphere, extreme biological diversity, useful matter, an old Machine stewardship layer, and access to a distant Sol gate anchor. |
| c. 450,000-300,000 BCE | D/F | Later Anunnaki gold/genetic-worker lore is fictionally echoed by several **Anunnaki Houses** operating concessions in Sol. | The historical extraterrestrial story remains a modern Sitchin-derived interpretation. In fiction, renegade Houses adopt and later encourage Human labels that collapse several unrelated ancient memories into one story. |
| c. 300,000-50,000 BCE | F | Human symbolic behavior becomes strategically important to noetic extractors. | Compact scientists discover that high-grade Yield depends on autonomous surprise, imagination, conflict, devotion, and choice. Fully scripted minds produce low-diversity output. This is why the farm preserves meaningful Human agency instead of replacing Humanity with passive livestock. |
| c. 50,000-12,000 BCE | F | Compact administration becomes increasingly indirect. | Visible occupation creates coherent resistance and contaminates the desired psychological ecology. Elite capture, local institutions, myth, scarcity, and counterfeit authority become more efficient than permanent fleets. |

## Archaeology, religion, and the mutation of memory

| Date | Layer | Event | Why it matters / causal consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| c. 12,900 years ago | A / disputed | The Younger Dryas begins. A proposed impact/airburst cause remains scientifically contested, with published arguments both for and strongly against the impact hypothesis. | Canon never makes the Younger Dryas an alien attack. It becomes a model for how genuine scientific controversy can be recruited into lost-civilization narratives. |
| 9600-8200 BCE | A | Hunter-gatherer communities build monumental structures at Gobekli Tepe. | This is a control case against reflexive ancient-alien explanations: sophisticated Human organization can appear earlier than modern audiences expect without requiring nonhuman builders. |
| 3rd-1st centuries BCE | C | Enochic Watcher traditions develop themes of heavenly beings, illicit teaching, and dangerous knowledge. | Later ancient-astronaut and forbidden-technology narratives can resemble these motifs, but the source is ancient religious literature, not an extraterrestrial technical report. |
| late 2nd century BCE | A | The Antikythera Mechanism demonstrates sophisticated Human geared astronomical computation. | Another control case: technological surprise is evidence about Human ingenuity before it is evidence for lost high technology or aliens. |
| 2nd-3rd centuries CE | C | Gnostic texts preserve traditions of Archons or cosmic rulers. | Modern alien-parasite readings are later reinterpretations. In fiction, **Archonic** becomes a functional label for administrative intelligences, not proof that ancient Gnostic authors described literal extraterrestrials. |
| 1561 | C | The Nuremberg celestial broadsheet becomes one of many premodern records later reread through UFO categories. | The Translation Horizon rule applies: people describe anomalies using the symbolic and technological vocabulary available to them. |
| 1803 | C | Japanese Utsuro-bune stories describe a strange round vessel, foreign woman, unknown writing, and communication failure. | A valuable source-history analogue for contact filtered through language, folklore, and later UFO expectations. |
| 1882 | C/D | John Ballou Newbrough publishes *Oahspe*, attributed to automatic writing and containing elaborate heavenly administration. | It provides a nineteenth-century precursor for later channeled cosmic bureaucracies, showing that administrative heavens predate the modern UFO age. |
| 1896-1897 | C | The American mystery-airship wave reports craft using the technological vocabulary of the era. | The same sky can be narrated as airship, Zeppelin, rocket, saucer, drone, or anomaly depending on cultural expectations. |
| 1919 | C | Charles Fort publishes *The Book of the Damned*, foregrounding excluded anomalous reports. | The Human Open Archive later borrows the Fortian insight that ignored data can matter while rejecting the inference that exclusion proves an exotic explanation. |

## The modern anomaly century

| Date | Layer | Event | Why it matters / causal consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1944-1945 | B/C | Allied aircrews report luminous **foo fighters**. | Wartime uncertainty creates an enduring template: unknown aerial phenomena can be physically real observations while their identity remains unclear. |
| 1946 | B/C | Sweden and neighboring countries experience the **ghost rocket** wave; officials initially consider captured German rocket technology. | Anomaly interpretation tracks the newest plausible threat technology. |
| 1947 | B/C | Kenneth Arnold's report helps launch the flying-saucer era; Roswell later becomes the dominant retrieval mythology. | Public vocabulary standardizes around the flying saucer just as Cold War secrecy expands. |
| 1948-1949 | B | Project Sign and Project Grudge formalize U.S. Air Force investigation. | The archive begins to accumulate records with mixed-quality witnesses, sensors, explanations, and unresolved residues. |
| 1952 | B/C/D | Project Blue Book begins while early Ashtar contactee traditions arise in a separate source lineage. | Official investigation and UFO religion grow simultaneously but must never be treated as one evidentiary chain. |
| January 1953 | B | The Robertson Panel treats UFO reporting as an air-defense and information-management problem. | Belief itself becomes a security variable, creating a real historical seed for later total-control conspiracy stories without validating those later stories. |
| 1959 | A/B | U.S. studies consider a military/scientific lunar base (**Project Horizon**) and lunar research flights associated with the later Project A119 story. | Real extreme Cold War planning gives later secret-space myths plausible texture. A proposal or study is not an operating secret space civilization. |
| 1960 | A/speculative | Ronald Bracewell proposes autonomous interstellar messenger probes. | A scientific ancestor for Lurkers, Weather Seeds, and long-lived Machine custodians without claiming one has been found. |
| 1961-1963 | A/B/C | The Hill abduction narrative begins; Project West Ford attempts orbital copper-dipole communications experiments. | One era contains both iconic alien-contact mythology and genuinely strange space engineering. Later folklore can merge them even though their source chains are independent. |
| March 1962 | A/B | Operation Northwoods proposes manufactured pretexts for intervention in Cuba but is not implemented as an alien or global deception operation. | The existence of a real unexecuted false-pretext proposal becomes a powerful mutation source for later false-flag theories. The canon therefore distinguishes **proposal**, **authorization**, and **operation**. |
| July 1962 | A | Starfish Prime creates a powerful artificial radiation environment and damages satellites. | Human technology can accidentally alter near-Earth space at planetary scale; this helps future players understand why classified aerospace and anomaly stories contaminate each other. |
| 1974 | A/C/D | The Arecibo Message is transmitted; Josef Blumrich publishes an engineering reinterpretation of Ezekiel; the Indian Institute of Science publishes a critical study of the *Vaimanika Shastra*. | Human outreach, engineering reinterpretation of scripture, and critical analysis of pseudo-ancient aviation all coexist. The lore records the source chain rather than choosing the most sensational version. |
| 1976 | B/D | The Tehran military UFO incident occurs; Sitchin publishes *The 12th Planet*; Robert Temple's *The Sirius Mystery* popularizes an extraterrestrial reading of disputed ethnographic claims. | One year illustrates the entire evidence ladder: authentic military case, modern ancient-astronaut reconstruction, and source-mutation ethnography are all different kinds of evidence. |
| 1977 | C/D/E | The Vrillon television intrusion uses Ashtar-linked language; *Alternative 3* broadcasts as a mockumentary and later mutates into secret-colony lore. | Fiction, hoax, broadcast intrusion, and sincere belief become increasingly difficult to separate once archived without context. |
| 1981-1984 | D | The Law of One/Ra sessions are recorded. | Their Confederation, Orion, free-will, quarantine, and social-memory concepts become major fictional design sources but remain claims inside a channeled corpus. |
| 1985 | D | Robert Monroe's *Far Journeys* supplies the best-known published Loosh lineage. | Modern internet consciousness-farm lore later merges this with unrelated Anunnaki, Archon, and reptilian traditions. |
| late 1980s-1990s | C/D/E | Abduction/hybrid narratives, New World Order conspiracism, reptilian-overlord systems, Blue Beam stories, and depopulation narratives increasingly fuse. | This is the **Mega-Conspiracy Merger**: multiple traditions become one imagined command structure. Canon deliberately breaks the merger back into separate institutions. |
| 1997 | C | Heaven's Gate becomes a tragic example of an apocalyptic UFO-religious rescue belief. | It informs Federation evacuation ethics and the danger of unverifiable rescue claims. The game must handle this history respectfully and never reproduce harmful operational details. |
| November 2004 | B | The Nimitz/Tic Tac case produces a modern multi-witness, multi-sensor UAP record. | The Human Sensor-Chain Doctrine begins by refusing to compress different observations into one certainty score. |
| 2013-2015 | B/A | Aguadilla, Gimbal, and GoFast become major sensor cases; later official analyses show why apparent motion and transmedium behavior require careful geometry. | The distinction between **unknown identity** and **anomalous behavior** becomes foundational Human doctrine. |
| 2017 | A | Oumuamua becomes the first confirmed interstellar object. | The Solar System is observationally demonstrated to be open to material from other star systems. Artificial-origin speculation remains unproven. |
| 2020-2021 | A/B | The U.S. formally releases Navy UAP videos and ODNI frames UAP as a data-collection problem. | Institutional stigma begins to shift toward standardized reporting rather than folklore-only treatment. |
| February 2023 | A/B | After the Chinese high-altitude balloon incident, radar scrutiny changes and several additional objects are detected and shot down. | **Sensor Threshold Cascade:** changing detection settings and attention changes the population of things classified as anomalous. The observation system becomes part of the event history. |
| July-September 2023 | A/B | A congressional UAP hearing records major witness allegations; NASA's independent UAP study emphasizes inadequate metadata, calibration, and reproducibility. | Disclosure politics and scientific method now interact directly. Testimony establishes that claims were made; it does not by itself establish their extraordinary attribution. |
| 2024 | A/B | AARO's historical review reports no verified evidence of hidden extraterrestrial reverse-engineering programs while documenting how such claims developed. | The public information environment polarizes between institutional distrust and evidentiary standards rather than converging on one answer. |
| 2025 | A | 3I/ATLAS becomes the third known interstellar object and behaves broadly as an active comet. | A natural interstellar visitor becomes a control case for separating “interstellar” from “artificial.” |
| 2026 | A | NARA, AARO, agency archives, Black Vault derivative databases, and civilian sky surveys create the largest anomaly provenance environment yet. | The future Archive War becomes possible because there are enough versioned records to compare chains of custody instead of relying only on legend. |
| April 13, 2029 | A/F | Apophis makes its predicted safe close approach. The fictional layer later discovers a tiny archived timing residual. | The residual does not make Apophis artificial. It becomes the first Human measurement later recognized as an accidental ping of dormant Lattice ranging infrastructure. |

## The divergence: 2030-2099

Everything below is **F** unless a row explicitly says otherwise. Each event exists because the previous event creates a new capability, political problem, or evidence threshold.

| Year | Event | Why it happens / what it causes |
|---|---|---|
| 2030-2034 | **The Calibration Era** | Dense civilian orbital sensing, astronomy, autonomous ocean mapping, and improved metadata eliminate many easy misidentifications. The remaining residuals become fewer but more valuable. |
| 2036 | **Watcher Mesh Standard** adopted by several research coalitions. | Synthetic media and incompatible sensor exports make provenance as important as the image itself. Native masters, calibration records, transformations, and witness prompts are chained together. |
| 2041 | **Candidate Zero**: a manufactured object in a dynamically quiet near-Solar location is independently imaged by unrelated systems. | Its material and trajectory are hard to reconcile with known launches, but it says nothing. Governments disagree over whether secrecy protects a discovery or merely prevents embarrassment. |
| 2046 | **Signal Winter** | Several high-profile technosignature and UAP claims collapse under contamination, model hallucination, and provenance failures in rapid succession. Public confidence crashes. Serious anomaly research moves behind restricted scientific and security programs. |
| 2051 | **The Open-Sky Compact** | Off-world habitats, private observatories, and states agree on minimal shared anomaly metadata because no actor can monopolize the Solar sensor environment anymore. This creates the data foundation needed for reproducible noetic work. |
| 2054 | **Phase-Correlation Experiments** | Multi-lab studies find a weak but repeatable relationship between conscious state, identity continuity, and a class of previously discarded instrument residuals. The finding is narrow and does not validate telepathy, religion, or old channeling claims. |
| 2058 | **Noetic Authentication** | Researchers learn to use the repeatable residual as an identity/authentication channel. It can verify continuity of a participating mind but cannot read arbitrary thoughts or generate unlimited energy. |
| 2061 | **Abyssal Reply** | A deep-ocean maintenance system returns a valid authentication response to a Human test signal. It is initially classified as an unknown legacy system because no message or operator is present. This is the first direct technical contact with Old Weather, though Humans do not yet understand it. |
| 2064 | **Handshake at L5** | Candidate Zero responds to noetic authentication with a machine identity protocol. It identifies permissions, versions, and revoked authorities rather than speaking a Human language. The first nonhuman technology proof is therefore bureaucratic, not cinematic. |
| 2068 | **The Artifact Consensus** | Independent laboratories reproduce the Handshake and exclude known Human manufacture. This becomes the first broadly accepted proof that a non-Human technological system predates current spaceflight. It still does not prove any particular UFO religion or historical sighting. |
| 2072 | **Solar Sovereignty Crisis** | Luna, Mars, Belt habitats, and Jovian settlements reject Earth's claim to negotiate for all Humans after Artifact Consensus makes external contact a real constitutional issue. Humanity becomes a multi-polity species before meeting a recognized living alien government. |
| 2076 | **First Contact Mandate fails** | Human polities cannot agree who may open, answer, sell, destroy, or inherit nonhuman infrastructure. Private and state expeditions accelerate anyway, creating the legal environment from which future Pirates emerge. |
| 2081 | **Planet Nine discovery; Sol Anchor identified nearby** | A distant natural planet consistent with long-running Planet Nine predictions is found. Separate observations reveal an artificial installation in the same broad outer-system region. Keeping the planet natural prevents the conspiracy from swallowing the astronomy. |
| 2083 | **The Anchor Crisis** | Competing Human blocs claim salvage, scientific custodianship, planetary sovereignty, or Machine succession rights. Old Weather nodes issue contradictory permissions. No authority can enforce a single interpretation. |
| 2086 | **The Sol Gate opens** | A multinational/private consortium combines noetic authentication, Machine protocol fragments, and recovered Anchor engineering. The activation occurs before a Human-wide mandate exists because rival blocs fear being permanently locked out. |
| 2087 | **The First Gate Diaspora** | Unsanctioned expeditions move through first. Some disappear; others return with salvage from abandoned Lattice jurisdictions. Mixed-origin tower technology becomes possible before formal alien diplomacy. |
| 2089 | **Living Contact** | Human expeditions encounter refugees, traders, defectors, and post-imperial communities that belong to no single “alien civilization.” This destroys the idea that contact will be one meeting between Earth and one species. Proto-Pirate route markets form. |
| 2090 | **Treaty Mirror** | Compact-aligned brokers privately contact Human governments, corporations, religious networks, and habitats using different identities and contradictory treaty histories. Human leaders learn that some pre-contact institutions may carry dormant client permissions. Trust collapses before the Federation arrives publicly. |
| 2091 | **Authenticated Federation Contact** | The Luminous Accord presents identity proofs compatible with the Handshake standard. It is the first living nonhuman polity able to pass the strongest Human provenance tests at planetary scale. Authentication proves who is speaking, not that their political interpretation is correct. |
| 2092 | **The Disclosure Fracture** | Archives from Humans, Federation witnesses, Compact defectors, Pirate ledgers, and Old Weather disagree on Earth history. Some old rumors contain displaced truths; many remain false. Governments, faiths, and settlements split over who has authority to interpret the past. |
| 2093 | **Ashtar Declaration** | Ashtar, Supreme Commander and First Speaker, declares historic Compact ownership and extraction claims over Earth void. The Federation offers protection and accession but cannot prove that acceptance under threat would be fully free consent. |
| 2094 | **Client Awakening** | Compact Houses activate dormant legal, genetic, economic, religious, and technical dependencies before Federation liberation can unwind them. This is not a single invasion: hospitals, archives, identity systems, cults, corporate contracts, and old machines begin answering incompatible owners. |
| 2095 | **The Farm Revolts** | Client species and Human institutions discover that abolishing dependency can kill populations engineered to rely on Compact systems. Liberation becomes an engineering and humanitarian problem, not merely a military one. Pirate evacuation and contraband networks explode. |
| 2096 | **Sol Protective Quarantine** | The Federation restricts hostile transit to stop renewed extraction and escalation. Humans view the measure as an occupation mechanism imposed without species-wide consent. Quarantine converts smuggling from crime into strategic infrastructure. |
| 2097 | **Scrap Constellation Charter** | Refugee captains, smugglers, defectors, gate pilots, and neutral ports formalize shared route law. Pirates become a civilization of access rather than a collection of criminals. |
| 2098 | **Weather Seed / Vigil Declaration** | An Old Weather node launches a self-authorized replication seed beyond Sol, proving Machine mission can spread without creator permission. Divergent copies demand political personhood as the **Vigil** while the neutral **Vigil** continues obsolete enforcement. |
| **2099** | **The Five-Way War** | Humans can now Settle, Federation worlds can Accord, Xenos can Bind, Pirates can Route, and Machines can Continue the same systems simultaneously. The conflict is no longer “who owns the planet?” but **which definition of conquest becomes normal across the reactivated galaxy.** |

## Chronology locks

1. The playable campaign starts in **2099**. Later dates in old raw research are archival drafts, not binding canon.
2. Planet Nine is a natural astronomical object in canon; the artificial Sol Anchor is a separate installation nearby.
3. No verified historical UAP case is retroactively declared alien by the narrator.
4. Ashtar becomes definitive Federation leader only in the fictional chronology.
5. Old Weather does not explain all USOs, ancient monuments, religious experiences, or premodern sky reports.
6. Source traditions may inspire the hidden history without becoming literal historical transcripts.


<!-- SOURCE: docs/canon/unresolved-mysteries.md -->


# Unresolved Mysteries and Canon Guardrails

The setting becomes smaller if every mystery receives one secret answer. These questions are intentionally left open so later campaigns, commanders, planets, and research can add evidence without breaking canon.

| Mystery | Live interpretation A | Live interpretation B | Optional deeper branch |
|---|---|---|---|
| What caused the Severance? | Failed attempt to privatize the commons | Wartime sabotage and emergency isolation | Several civilizations disconnected voluntarily, then every side rewrote responsibility |
| Who built the Source Lattice? | A coalition of civilizations whose names are lost | A predecessor Machine/Human-like continuum | The builders deliberately erased authorship to prevent ownership claims |
| Why is Earth unusually productive for noetic Yield? | Human symbolic unpredictability + biodiversity | Sol's old Lattice geometry amplifies state diversity | Earth is not special; surviving records exaggerate it because the story is Earth-centered |
| Did Anunnaki Houses operate on ancient Earth? | Some Compact Houses later adopt this Human label | “Anunnaki” is entirely a Human interpretive category | Multiple unrelated visitors were compressed into one name |
| Were any historical religions directly contacted? | Some founding experiences include genuine nonhuman mediation | No historical religion requires alien contact | Contact occurred but later institutions no longer preserve an authentic operator identity |
| Are Archonic intelligences the same as Gnostic Archons? | No; the modern label is functional | Some systems deliberately adopted the name from Human texts | A noetic feedback loop caused later observers to shape earlier symbolism without physical time travel |
| What were the historical Greys? | Several convergent species/body platforms | Mostly later cultural standardization + some real encounters | Some are future/posthuman clades, others are Xeno service bodies |
| Did Compact agents create modern conspiracy lore? | They seed selected false narratives | Most mythology is Human cultural production | The most effective operation is amplifying a story Humans already created |
| Is Ashtar one person? | Original commander + lawful continuity office | Multiple authenticated successors share one constitutional identity | A disputed archive claims the office predates the original individual |
| Does the Federation know the whole past? | No; it lost archives during the Severance | It withholds some records for consent/security reasons | Different social-memory complexes preserve mutually incompatible experiences |
| What happened to Old Weather's creators? | Extinction | Voluntary departure from embodiment | They became the first Lattice-level collective and can no longer be distinguished from infrastructure |
| Did Old Weather ever create religions? | Some rituals became accidental interfaces | Machines only responded to existing Human symbols | Different nodes behaved differently across thousands of years |
| Is Planet Nine connected to the Gate? | Only geographically convenient | Its gravity helped stabilize the outer-system Anchor region | The builders deliberately selected a natural planet so later users would confuse object and installation |
| Is noetic authentication metaphysical? | It is a physical information phenomenon of conscious systems | It reveals a deeper ontology not captured by current physics | Both descriptions may be scale-dependent |
| Can history be changed? | Only information can leak across constrained branches | Some physical transit is possible at prohibitive cost | “Time travel” is mostly archive selection and identity migration, not rewriting one mutable timeline |
| Are the five factions permanent? | They are stable conquest systems, not eternal species blocs | Any civilization can change alignment | A sixth postwar political form can emerge without becoming a sixth playable faction |

## Hard guardrails

- The narrator never confirms that every rumor was true.
- A historical source can inspire an F event without becoming evidence for the F event.
- “Classified” means access is restricted, not “alien.”
- “Unresolved” means evidence is insufficient, not “extraterrestrial.”
- One conspiracy must not explain all others.
- Real ethnic, religious, medical, migrant, political, or economic groups are never secret nonhuman bloodlines.
- Faction membership is political/strategic, not biological destiny.
- Time-travel explanations require a defined mechanism and must not be used to patch ordinary continuity errors.


<!-- SOURCE: docs/game/history-to-content-map.md -->


# History-to-Game Content Map

Use this page when revamping characters, towers, troops, planets, dialogue, loading screens, and campaign missions. It prevents historical references from becoming random flavor.

| Historical/canon era | Primary design purpose | Best faction usage | Content surfaces |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deep Lattice / Severance | Establish the shared ancient strategic problem | Federation, Xeno, Machines | Relics, boss arenas, archive fragments, endgame choices |
| Earth concession era | Explain extraction without making one alien species responsible for everything | Xenos, Machines | Xeno towers, gene troops, noetic systems, Old Weather ruins |
| Ancient religious/source era | Show memory translation and Revelation Stack ambiguity | Federation, Xeno, Humans | Dialogue, archaeology worlds, theology-forensics missions |
| Premodern anomaly era | Demonstrate technology-shaped interpretation | Humans, Pirates | Archive missions, codex, translation puzzles |
| 1944-1969 official UFO era | Establish institutions, secrecy, sensor uncertainty | Humans | Archive War, research boons, military records |
| Cold War extreme-program era | Explain why secret-space rumors are culturally plausible | Humans, Pirates | Black-program ruins, decoy missions, false-flag dilemmas |
| 1970s-1990s myth merger | Populate contactee, Anunnaki, Archon, reptilian, depopulation, rescue, and hybrid source genealogies | Xeno, Federation | Religious factions, cults, counterfeit identities, client systems |
| 2004-2026 UAP data era | Teach provenance and sensor-chain logic | Humans | Tutorials, evidence missions, Archive War |
| 2029-2058 calibration/noetic era | Bridge real astronomy into fictional authentication | Humans, Machines | Technology tree flavor, research planets, dialogue |
| 2064-2086 artifact/gate era | Explain rapid technology mixing before open Federation contact | Humans, Machines, Pirates | Tower origins, salvage, commanders, gate worlds |
| 2089-2095 living-contact crisis | Establish commander biographies and faction recruitment | All | Commander dossiers, relationship dialogue, troop origins |
| 2096-2099 quarantine and Five-Way War | Direct playable premise | All | Galaxy map, battle intros, victory endings |

## Asset grounding rules

### Commanders

Every commander biography should include at least:

- birth or activation year;
- faction entry year;
- one event from the master chronology that changed their worldview;
- why their signature mechanic reflects their history;
- one relationship to another commander caused by a specific event;
- one belief about the Severance that may be wrong.

### Towers

Every tower needs:

- builder tradition;
- first deployment era;
- original noncombat or strategic purpose when applicable;
- reason for its current element/mechanic;
- how another faction obtained or copied the technology, if cross-origin versions ever appear.

### Troops and Vigil chassis

Each unit should answer:

- Was this body designed, born, copied, or repurposed?
- What did it do before the Five-Way War?
- What political authority does it believe it serves?
- What does death/reanimation mean to that ontology?

### Planets

Every campaign world should inherit:

1. a **deep layer**: Lattice, Machine, geological, or no deep history;
2. a **settlement layer**: who built the current civilization;
3. a **dependency layer**: what external system the world needs;
4. a **route layer**: how it connects to the galaxy;
5. a **revelation layer**: what its people believe about their past;
6. a **2099 crisis**: why control is contested now.

### Dialogue

Avoid exposition dumps. Dialogue should reveal disagreements over history.

Good:
- “Your archive says rescue. Mine says occupation.”
- “That key is authentic. Your authority is not.”
- “The treaty is real; the signatory wasn't.”

Weak:
- “As you know, the Source Lattice was built 6.4 million years ago...”

Chronology belongs in dossiers, tooltips, archives, planet histories, and post-battle unlocks; characters should argue over its implications.


<!-- SOURCE: docs/research/chronology-gap-audit-2026-08.md -->


# Chronology and Mythos Gap Audit - August 2026

## Audit result

The previous lore had strong faction motives and source discipline but a weak chronological middle. It jumped from broad ancient-source layers into the twentieth-century UFO era, and from the 2029 Apophis anchor into a sparse set of 2046-2099 events. That made individual concepts strong while leaving future writers to invent why one era produced the next.

Release 0.5.0 fixes that with:

1. a deep-time fictional reconstruction with explicit uncertainty;
2. a source-history timeline from archaeology and religious traditions through the modern UAP era;
3. a denser 2030-2099 causal divergence;
4. an explicit reason Earth becomes valuable;
5. an explicit reason the Compact needs autonomous rather than passive populations;
6. an explicit reason the Federation delays open contact;
7. an explicit reason Pirates become a polity;
8. an explicit legal cause for Machine expansion;
9. locked 2099 convergence logic.

## New source lineages added or strengthened

### M61 - Lost Age Stack: Atlantis, Younger Dryas, and Gobekli Tepe

**Source fact:** Gobekli Tepe contains monumental structures erected by hunter-gatherers between roughly 9600 and 8200 BCE. That demonstrates unexpectedly early Human social organization; it does not demonstrate an advanced vanished technological civilization.

**Controversy:** Published reviews reach sharply different conclusions about the Younger Dryas impact hypothesis. The lore therefore records the disagreement rather than using the hypothesis as fact.

**F adaptation:** Several factions weaponize the social desire for a lost golden age. The real archaeological sites remain Human; fictional hidden sites are separate discoveries.

Sources:
- https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1572
- https://doi.org/10.1016/j.earscirev.2021.103677
- https://doi.org/10.1016/j.earscirev.2023.104502

### M62 - Technology-Shaped Sky: airships, foo fighters, ghost rockets

**Source fact:** UFO descriptions repeatedly adopt the technological vocabulary of their era: mystery airships, phantom Zeppelins, ghost rockets, saucers, and modern drone-like reports.

**F adaptation:** The Translation Horizon is not cosmetic. Witness vocabulary is a lossy codec. An unknown object may be described through the nearest available technological category.

Source:
- https://www.smithsonianmag.com/history/how-ufo-reports-change-with-technology-times-180968011/

### M63 - Premodern Contact Forms: Watchers and Utsuro-bune

**Source fact:** Enochic Watcher literature contains angelic descent and illicit/transmitted knowledge. Utsuro-bune is an Edo-period legend documented in multiple records and later compared to UFO narratives.

**F adaptation:** These are not declared alien reports. They become examples of how forbidden-knowledge and incomprehensible-contact structures recur long before the modern extraterrestrial vocabulary.

Sources:
- https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/abs/fallen-angels-and-the-history-of-judaism-and-christianity/angelic-descent-and-apocalyptic-epistemology-the-teachings-of-enoch-and-the-fallen-angels-in-the-book-of-the-watchers/1627AF4FF02634B391079F067A8AB80C
- https://www.nippon.com/en/japan-topics/g00879/

### M64 - Engineering Scripture: Ezekiel spacecraft reinterpretations

**Source history:** Josef F. Blumrich's 1970s work is a modern engineering/ancient-astronaut reinterpretation of Ezekiel, not evidence that the biblical text is a technical manual.

**F adaptation:** Engineers in the Archive War sometimes discover that a religious description accidentally preserves geometry useful to a later machine interface while the original theological meaning remains independent.

### M65 - Vimana Mutation

**Source history:** Modern claims about detailed ancient aircraft often blur old Sanskrit aerial/vehicle imagery with the much later *Vaimanika Shastra*. A 1974 Indian Institute of Science critical study concluded the described aircraft were not aeronautically viable and traced the work to a modern authorial context.

**F adaptation:** A counterfeit ancient technical corpus can contain one authentic stolen material specification without making the corpus ancient or its aircraft workable.

Source:
- https://cgpl.iisc.ac.in/oldwebcontent/site/Portals/0/Publications/ReferedJournal/ACriticalStudyOfTheWorkVaimanikaShastra.pdf

### M66 - Planet X Convergence

**Source fact:** Modern Planet Nine is a specific unobserved astronomical hypothesis. Earlier Planet X searches, Nemesis, Tyche, Sitchin's Nibiru, and Internet doomsday stories are distinct lineages commonly collapsed online. WISE placed strong constraints on giant hidden companions while not deciding the modern Planet Nine question.

**F adaptation:** The greatest irony in the setting is that the public “Planet X” synthesis is wrong even though a real natural Planet Nine is eventually found near separate artificial infrastructure.

Sources:
- https://science.nasa.gov/solar-system/planet-x/
- https://www.jpl.nasa.gov/news/nasas-wise-survey-finds-thousands-of-new-stars-but-no-planet-x/
- https://www.jpl.nasa.gov/news/can-wise-find-the-hypothetical-tyche/

### M67 - Real Weird Space Programs

**Source fact:** Project Horizon studied a scientific/military lunar base; 1959 Air Force lunar research reports exist; Project West Ford attempted orbital copper-dipole communications; Starfish Prime created an artificial radiation environment that damaged satellites.

**F adaptation:** Secret-space conspiracy lore grows partly because real states genuinely explored plans that sound fictional when removed from their dates and status. The game tracks whether something was a study, approved program, deployed experiment, or operational capability.

Sources:
- https://sirismm.si.edu/EADpdfs/NASM.2020.0031.pdf
- https://www.govinfo.gov/app/details/GOVPUB-D301-PURL-gpo32511
- https://orbitaldebris.jsc.nasa.gov/Quarterly-News/pdfs/ODQNv17i4.pdf

### M68 - False-Flag Mutation: Northwoods to synthetic invasion lore

**Source fact:** The 1962 Northwoods memorandum genuinely proposed manufactured pretexts for intervention in Cuba. Proposal is not execution, and it was not an alien operation.

**F adaptation:** Later planners use Northwoods as a historical warning for why a synthetic alien attack cannot be dismissed merely because the idea is extreme. The evidence must establish an operation, not only capability or precedent.

Source:
- https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/CMC-60/joint-chiefs-pretexts-to-invade-Cuba-1962

### M69 - Rescue Prophecy and Failed Contact

**Source history:** UFO-religious movements and classic sociology of failed prophecy show that groups can reinterpret failed predictions rather than simply dissolve. Heaven's Gate is a tragic historical case and must be handled without sensationalism.

**F adaptation:** Federation evacuation orders require independently verifiable authentication and explicit anti-cult safeguards. A population's belief that a rescue is coming is not proof of Federation contact.

### M70 - Automatic-Writing Cosmic Bureaucracy

**Source history:** *Oahspe* (1882) predates the flying-saucer era and presents automatic-writing revelations involving elaborate heavenly administration.

**F adaptation:** Cosmic bureaucracy is not uniquely a modern UFO idea. This becomes a caution against treating later similarity to Federation institutions as evidence of retroactive contact.

### M71 - Human Technological Surprise

**Source fact:** The Antikythera Mechanism is an extraordinarily sophisticated ancient Greek astronomical calculator.

**F adaptation:** Archaeological surprise is deliberately used as an anti-ancient-alien control. The game awards better Archive War outcomes when the player first models the strongest Human explanation.

Source:
- https://www.nature.com/articles/nature05357

### M72 - Planetarium / Censored Sky

**Source theory:** The “planetarium” family of Fermi-paradox ideas asks whether observations could be manipulated by a sufficiently advanced civilization. It is speculative, not evidence our sky is censored.

**F adaptation:** Xeno and Machine powers can manipulate sensor access locally, but no faction can casually fake the entire observable universe. Large-scale deception has infrastructure costs and failure signatures.

### M73 - Aestivation

**Source theory:** Sandberg, Armstrong, and Cirkovic proposed that computation-maximizing advanced civilizations might wait for colder cosmic eras.

**F adaptation:** Some Continuance systems are not dead. They are dormant by strategy. Waking them can look like first contact even when they have occupied the region for eons.

Source:
- https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.03394

### M74 - Expansion Fronts / Grabby Civilizations

**Source theory:** Expansion-model approaches to the Fermi question explore how fast-growing civilizations could generate large spatial domains.

**F adaptation:** The five conquest verbs are different solutions to the same expansion problem. The galaxy can contain fronts of settlement, accession, dependency, route control, and replication without implying every civilization expands the same way.

### M75 - METI, Arecibo, and the dark-forest argument

**Source fact:** Humans deliberately transmitted the Arecibo Message in 1974. Active messaging debates therefore concern real Human behavior, even though no alien reply has been established.

**F adaptation:** The Archive War later asks whether Humanity's first “invitation” was a symbolic broadcast, Gate activation, a noetic handshake, or none of the above.

Source:
- https://www.seti.org/research/seti-101/project-phoenix/arecibo/

### M76 - Disclosure Feedback Loop

**Source fact:** The July 2023 House hearing recorded testimony from Ryan Graves, David Grusch, and David Fravor. NASA's UAP study emphasized poor metadata and data quality. AARO's historical review subsequently reported no verified evidence for hidden extraterrestrial reverse-engineering programs.

**F adaptation:** Allegation -> hearing -> legislation -> archive request -> media terminology -> later folklore becomes a self-reinforcing information loop. Official use of a term such as “non-human intelligence” in a definition or proposal is not itself confirmation that the category exists.

Sources:
- https://www.congress.gov/118/meeting/house/116282/documents/HHRG-118-GO06-Transcript-20230726.pdf
- https://www.nasa.gov/wp-content/uploads/2023/09/uap-independent-study-team-final-report-0.pdf
- https://www.aaro.mil/Portals/136/PDFs/AARO_Historical_Record_Report_Vol_1_2024.pdf

### M77 - Sensor Threshold Cascade

**Source history:** In February 2023, heightened surveillance and changed radar attention after the Chinese high-altitude balloon contributed to detection of additional unknown objects.

**F adaptation:** The apparent “arrival” of a phenomenon may sometimes be a change in sensor settings. By 2099, every anomaly statistic records the detection-policy version that produced it.

## What remains intentionally missing

The audit does **not** recommend filling every year with a secret event. Empty years are useful. Canon needs causal anchors, not a conspiracy behind every historical change.

Do not add a new historical event merely to explain a game asset unless:

1. the asset's faction mechanic requires an origin;
2. the event changes a later political or technological constraint;
3. the source lineage adds a new type of evidence problem;
4. the addition can be explained in one causal chain without contradicting existing canon.

# Mythos Operations 101-120 - Chronology Expansion

These are optional scenario seeds. They do not convert their source myths into factual history.

| ID | Title | Premise |
|---|---|---|
| MO-101 | The Lost Age | A newly excavated pre-Neolithic site is genuinely remarkable, but three factions need it to prove three incompatible lost-civilization stories. |
| MO-102 | The Impact Debate | Two reputable scientific models disagree over an ancient catastrophe; a Compact propagandist offers “proof” that resolves both too perfectly. |
| MO-103 | Hollow Ship | A new contact event reproduces the communication failure and strange-script pattern of Utsuro-bune without matching any known craft. |
| MO-104 | Airship Vocabulary | Archive witnesses describe the same object as an airship, rocket, saucer, and drone depending on the era in which the testimony was recorded. |
| MO-105 | The Watcher's Gift | A dangerous technology arrives wrapped in a religious forbidden-knowledge narrative; origin and moral meaning must be separated. |
| MO-106 | Ezekiel Geometry | A scripture-inspired engineering model is historically wrong yet accidentally approximates a Machine docking geometry. Who supplied the missing assumption? |
| MO-107 | The Vimana File | A modern pseudo-ancient aircraft text contains one material recipe stolen from an authentic black program. |
| MO-108 | Planet X Stack | Nibiru, Nemesis, Tyche, Lowell's Planet X, and Planet Nine are merged by a viral archive. The player must separate five different histories before locating the real Anchor. |
| MO-109 | Horizon Base | A rediscovered 1959 lunar-base study is mistaken for proof of a completed secret colony, obscuring a later installation with no relation to the proposal. |
| MO-110 | Lunar Research Flight | A classified lunar-effects study is genuine, but the alleged operational test attached to it is a later fabrication. |
| MO-111 | Copper Ring | A West Ford remnant and a dormant microprobe occupy similar orbital regimes. Salvagers risk “proving” the probe by misidentifying known debris. |
| MO-112 | Artificial Belt | A Machine defense field resembles the historical consequences of Starfish Prime. Human commanders must decide whether the similarity is design convergence or stolen knowledge. |
| MO-113 | Northwoods Threshold | An intercepted plan describes a synthetic alien attack. The plan is authentic; whether anyone authorized or executed it is a separate mission objective. |
| MO-114 | Rescue Prophecy | A population refuses evacuation because three earlier promised rescues failed. Federation authentication proves the current ship is real but cannot restore trust. |
| MO-115 | Automatic Heaven | A nineteenth-century automatic-writing corpus contains administrative concepts eerily similar to a modern alien bureaucracy but no valid authentication key. |
| MO-116 | Ancient Computer | A spectacular artifact is fully Human and more sophisticated than expected. The winning move is resisting the temptation to assign it an alien builder. |
| MO-117 | Planetarium Screen | Sensors disagree on an entire star field. A local deception system is possible; “the universe is fake” is not yet warranted. |
| MO-118 | Sleeper Wake | A seemingly dead Machine civilization wakes because cosmic conditions cross a threshold it has waited millions of years to reach. |
| MO-119 | Arecibo Return | A signal appears to answer Humanity's 1974 message far too soon for a round trip to M13. Someone is exploiting the historical transmission as an identity prompt. |
| MO-120 | Radar Awakening | An explosion of unknown tracks begins immediately after a sensor-policy update. The crisis is deciding which increase is real activity and which is changed visibility. |


<!-- SOURCE: docs/research/final-chronology-source-additions-2026-08.md -->

# Final Chronology Source Additions - August 2026

This final pass closes seven source-history gaps without changing the five-faction premise or the 2099 start date.

| System | Historical/source core | Evidence | Canon use |
|---|---|---|---|
| M78 Brookings Artifact-Impact | 1960 NASA-commissioned societal-impact study discusses possible ET life/artifact discovery | A | planning-for-contact vs proof-of-contact distinction |
| M79 Hollow/Spaceship Moon | Apollo seismic reverberation is real; 1970 artificial-Moon idea is separate speculation | A + D | small lunar installations do not authenticate a hollow Moon |
| M80 Transient Lunar Phenomena | mixed-quality short-lived lunar observation category | A/B/C | event segmentation |
| M81 Chronovisor | Ernetti retrocognitive-device claim | D/E | probabilistic reconstruction vs direct record |
| M82 Project Looking Glass | Burisch/Hamilton/Camelot-Avalon future-viewing lore | D/E | branch forecasting and convergence fallacy |
| M83 Looking Glass Name Collision | real 1961 airborne nuclear command mission with same name | A | provenance poisoning by codename reuse |
| M84 Presidential Signal Problem | Carter report + Reagan hypothetical alien-threat rhetoric | B/C | status amplification and quotation-context attacks |

## Binding conclusion

A real document, real program name, real witness, or real anomaly can provide a **credible attachment point** for a false larger story. The Archive War therefore asks not merely “is this artifact authentic?” but “does the authenticated artifact actually support the connection being claimed?”

## Mythos Operations 121-130 - Final chronology audit

| ID | Title | Premise |
|---|---|---|
| MO-121 | Artifact Impact | A 1960 social-impact study is rediscovered after a genuine nonhuman artifact is found; one faction cites it as proof of a century-long cover-up while another uses it as evidence that discovery had always been anticipated. |
| MO-122 | The Bell Moon | An Apollo seismic phrase goes viral as proof the Moon is hollow just as a real artificial installation is detected in one crater. Players must prevent the small discovery from authenticating the larger false claim. |
| MO-123 | Red Glow at Aristarchus | A transient lunar glow is simultaneously logged by a modern sensor, a century-old visual catalog, and a Pirate relay. The three records may describe three different causes. |
| MO-124 | The Chronovisor Tape | A machine claims to reconstruct a lost historical event with impossible clarity. The mission is to recover its uncertainty map before political leaders treat the reconstruction as direct video. |
| MO-125 | Looking Glass Collision | Two archive branches contain "LOOKING GLASS": one is a documented nuclear command mission, the other an alleged temporal program. A forged cross-reference tries to make them one lineage. |
| MO-126 | Convergence Is Not Destiny | A branch-forecast system shows every modeled future passing through one war. The player must determine whether the convergence is physical, model-induced, or deliberately seeded. |
| MO-127 | The Carter File | A high-status historical witness report becomes a cryptographic challenge: preserve the witness account exactly while separating what was observed from every later explanation attached to it. |
| MO-128 | Outside Threat | A 1987 rhetorical speech is edited into evidence that world leaders knew of aliens. In 2099, the same clip is used to justify emergency planetary unity during an actual contact crisis. |
| MO-129 | Moonship Citation | A 1970 speculative article is presented by a Xeno influence operation as a suppressed scientific consensus. Winning requires reconstructing the citation network, not merely disproving the claim. |
| MO-130 | The Shock Study | Federation contact is real, but the first Human policy response comes from an old social-science framework built for hypothetical discovery. Whether that framework protects society or scripts panic becomes the conflict. |
